# Vision Fidelity Ledger — v7 (2026-10-05) — MILESTONE M1 re-run

**Supersedes v6 as the current assessment.** v6 is kept whole at `VISION_FIDELITY_LEDGER_v6.md` (its
Tier-1 entries are the ones P1.17 closed and the register rows cite), as v3 is at `VISION_FIDELITY_LEDGER_v3.md`.
This edition re-measures the product after P1.17, the second truth pass, as the M1 line requires — the Tier-1
count is measured again, never declared: a fresh six-region assessment against HEAD `6e63e762` (port :8031,
single-user mode, `AI_DISABLE_LOCAL=1`). Under that flag the gateway routes every model call to the
deterministic native floor — the configuration CI runs and the one any machine without a local model gets
(it is NOT the shipped default: with the flag unset and Ollama discoverable, the gateway serves from the
local model). What IS assessable on the floor is whether every floor-served surface discloses it.

**Tiers.** Each finding carries the tier the refuter stands behind — 1: a TRUTH DEFECT (a reached surface
tells the user something untrue, fabricates a figure, or certifies what it could not assess); 2: an
INVISIBLE SHORTFALL (partial, and the surface does not say so); 3: a CAPABILITY GAP that is disclosed or
not reached. DELIVERED entries carry no tier.

## How this document was generated — and what that means for reading it

Six assessors ran one vision region each against the booted HEAD, explicitly barred from three
sources: the vision's own §16, the previous edition of this ledger, and `AUTONOMOUS_PROGRESS.md`
(a record of intent, not proof). They executed routes, read handlers and components, and counted
stores. The ASSESSMENT was capped at ten findings per region, most consequential first — and every
region returned exactly ten, so **60 is the size of the cap, not the size of the gap**; a region's
eleventh-worst thing is not in this ledger. **Every finding was then attacked by an independent
refuter instructed to default to refuted** (v2 refuted six per region), who had to reproduce the gap (execute the
route, read the code, count the store) before letting it stand, and who was told to correct the
verdict UP or DOWN when the assessor had it wrong.

Reading rules that follow from the method:

1. **DELIVERED is understated by construction.** Assessors were told their job was the gap that
   remains, but to report DELIVERED where they verified it. Read the DELIVERED entries as the
   floor of what works, not the ceiling.
2. **Every one of the 60 findings was individually refuted.** The refuters reproduced
   47 as stated and overturned 13 — 3 moved to a
   HARSHER verdict (a DELIVERED claim that was not), 7 to a MILDER one (a STUB that was real
   machinery with an undisclosed shortfall). The verdict in each heading is the one the REFUTER
   stands behind; the assessor's original is shown where it differs.
2a. **And on the TIER, which is the axis M1 is scored on:** 1 finding(s) were made HARSHER by the refuter and 3 milder, with **1 escalated INTO tier 1** — the M1 measure itself. A refuter can tighten a tier while leaving the verdict untouched, and reporting only the verdict hid that: 0 of the escalations into tier 1 had the verdict axis call them MILDER.
3. **The floor is the environment.** A finding that says 'floor scaffold reached the user' is
   not a complaint that no model ran — it is a finding that the surface did not SAY so, or
   certified what it could not assess. That is the §15 principle-6 line, and it binds.
4. **Refuters reproduced with their OWN inputs.** Several verdicts below carry evidence from a
   second journey, VSB, change-record or transfer the refuter created on the live backend —
   a finding that reproduces under a second, independently chosen input is stronger evidence
   than one observation.

## R1 — §10 + §11 — the solution-quality bar, continuous compliance, and the faith-content constitution

**Assessor's region summary:** R1 (§10 the solution-quality bar, §11 continuous compliance and the faith-content constitution, plus QEP against Appendix A) measured by execution against the live backend at 127.0.0.1:8031 and by reading the handlers and components: a deliverable produced and exported, the QMS gate driven to both pass and fail, the federated compliance screen run, a full Genesis journey run, and fifteen QEP and Religion routes called including the deliberate failure cases (surah 1 ayah 9, transliterated recall input, translation of sacred text).  The headline is that §11's faith-content constitution is the strongest-held part of the system I examined, and it is held by execution, not by assertion. Arabic is fetched and labelled; a nonexistent ayah is refused 422 against the real ayah-count table; translation refuses 503 before any model call and the availability chip computes from the same probe; written recall refuses input it cannot compare and states in every payload that it says nothing about recitation; the recitation panel shows no score and derives every label from the retrieval response with no fallback copy of scripture; the QEP compliance audit returns compliant:null and names two controls it cannot run; analytics says measured:false; the scholar board says no scholars are verified. The A.11 tafsir defect (arabic_text, arabic_source and reference never reaching the screen) is closed. Genesis's §10 machinery genuinely models, simulates, screens and ranks, and on this floor-only deployment it withholds "modelled", "simulated", "optimised" and "ranked" with reasons that name what did not happen, and renders the non-comparison in place of the ranked cards. Nothing in A.9's six ratified refusals is missing: each one I probed refuses and says so, which is delivery.  The gap that remains is a small number of surfaces that fell out of that standard rather than a systemic failure. Three are tier 1. First and widest: the native floor's `_subject` label list omits "Brief" while `_CONTENT_LABELS` includes it, so every deliverable produced through /api/v1/deliverables/produce opens its Executive Summary — in the UI, in the stored record and in all four export formats — with "Subject:" followed by the platform's own house-style directive, two lines below the user's real brief; the same fallback makes a QEP tajwid lesson print "Terms most frequent in your request" over six terms lifted from the platform's prompt. Second: the Religion hub's "Generate Class Report" button serves a floor-composed Qur'an & Islamic Studies curriculum through the generic Education route with no provenance badge, no AI-assisted label and no teacher referral, on a hub where every sibling tool renders both a floor note and a disclaimer. Third: /api/v1/religion/interfaith is the one Religion handler with no disclaimer, no floor note and no withheld sections, returning a comparative-religion scholar persona over headings that purport to state three traditions' positions. The tier-2 items are an undisclosed diacritic sensitivity that marks a correct undiacriticised recall at 50%, a §10 bar that says "not measured by this gate" on a path that performs no modelling or ranking at all, a front-door card still promising halal certification guidance the route withholds, a roadmap card advertising the emotion inference A.9.4 refuses, and a toast promising a "QEP Season 2" that exists nowhere.  I did not hit the ten-finding cap, so this is the whole of what I found consequential in R1 — but it is the whole of R1, not the whole system: twelve of Appendix A's fifteen features have no module, and that is A.11's measured status and A.12's open Owner rulings rather than a defect to report here. Two things I verified as real that could easily have been constants: the QMS document-control audit_integrity of 1.0 is a genuine SHA3-512 recomputation that returns 0.0 with no verifiable history, and the XAI explanation runs the real MemorizationEngine and declares which of its weights is a fixed display constant.

### R1.0 · Every floor-served deliverable prints the platform's own house-style directive as its "Subject:", in the report body and in every downloaded file — **PARTIAL** · tier 1

- **severity (assessor):** high
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The sentence a reader is shown is "Subject: Lead with the decision and its cost, keep evidence tight, and prefer concrete numbers, timelines and owners over explanation of fundamentals. (domain: energy)." — the first line under the Executive Summary of a report whose brief was a Kenyan clinic losing vaccines. That is not an omission and not an environment fact: it is an affirmative statement naming the subject of the document, and it is false. The floor-provenance line directly above it ("composed by the deterministic native structured engine … arranges the headings it was asked for") discloses HOW the text was made but says nothing that makes this sentence true, so the disclosure does not pull it down to tier 2 or 3. The same root also makes the floor label its own prompt scaffolding as the learner's words (see `observed`), which is the same class of untruth on a QEP surface.
- **claim:** §10 — "Every solution is specifically designed … delivered end-to-end"; §15.6 "honesty over polish (never fabricate)". §13 — every deliverable is a living artefact the user exports.
- **observed:** POST /api/v1/deliverables/produce with brief "A 20-bed clinic in rural Kenya needs reliable power for vaccine refrigeration. Design and rank the best solution." returned content whose Executive Summary opens "Subject: Lead with the decision and its cost…" — the enterprise realm directive from agentic_core/taxonomy.py REALM_REGISTER, prepended to the prompt by agentic_core/api/deliverables.py:111-116. Cause: agentic_core/ai/native/engine.py:137 `_subject()` searches 17 labels (User, Problem, Challenge, Objective, Mission, Concept, Topic, …) and "Brief" is NOT among them, while `_CONTENT_LABELS` at line 149 DOES list "Brief" — the two lists disagree, exactly the disagreement the function's own comment at lines 134-136 records for "Mission" and fixed only there. With no match, `_subject` falls through to "the longest sentence" in the prompt, which for all four realms is the directive's second sentence. The identical fallback in `_content` (line 163: `return … if vals else prompt`) makes POST /api/v1/qep/tajweed/lesson {rule_name:"Idgham"} print "## Terms most frequent in your request" over "triggers pronunciation · transliteration common · educational assistant · assistant supporting · generate structured · pronunciation guide" — six terms the learner never typed, lifted from the platform's own prompt; POST /api/v1/qep/adaptation/execute does the same.
- **evidence:** GET /api/v1/deliverables/deliv-ad0fddc5/export (executed, HTTP 200) returns the false Subject line two lines below the true "> **Brief:** A 20-bed clinic in rural Kenya…", so the artefact contradicts itself on page one; the same string is in the stored record, in `versions[0].content`, and in the .pdf/.docx/.xlsx writers (agentic_core/api/deliverables.py:727, 758, 832). apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/Deliverables.tsx:320-323 renders `selected.content` verbatim. Reproduced on a second surface: /api/v1/qep/tajweed/lesson, /api/v1/qep/adaptation/execute.
- **disclosed to the user:** No. The surface discloses that the floor composed the text; it nowhere discloses that the "Subject:" line is the platform's own instruction rather than the user's subject, or that the "terms most frequent in your request" are not from the request.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). NOT REFUTED — reproduced verbatim, and larger than filed. Tier 1 is right and the assessor's own tier reasoning holds: the line is an affirmative statement naming the document's subject, and it is false — it is the platform's house-style instruction to itself. The floor-provenance line above it ("composed by the deterministic native structured engine … arranges the headings it was asked for") discloses HOW the text was made and says nothing that makes the Subject sentence true, so no disclosure pulls it to tier 2; and it is an assertion, not an omission, so it cannot fall to tier 3. Three corrections, all making it worse, none changing the tier: (a) the false subject is not confined to the Executive Summary — it is re-printed as the stated subject of EVERY section ("Native structured frame for 'Background' over: Lead with the decision and its cost…", likewise Analysis, Findings, Recommendations); (b) the exporters are four not three — PDF at deliverables.py:728, DOCX at 759, PPTX at 795 and XLSX at 838 each write d["content"] verbatim immediately after writing the true "Brief:" line, so every downloaded format contradicts itself on page one; (c) the same root defect leaks the raw prompt into the body of other floor surfaces — POST /api/v1/religion/halal-review prints "Native structured frame for 'Manufacturing Considerations' over: Product: Gelatin gummy … Conduct a pre-assessment and provide: ## Cross-Contamination Risks ## Manufacturing Consid", truncated mid-word, as its subject. Verdict PARTIAL stands: the run does produce a real deliverable with correctly grounded term extraction (vaccine refrigeration, rural kenya), so it is not a STUB; the defect is a truth defect inside working output.
- **refuter's evidence:** POST /api/v1/deliverables/produce with the Kenyan-clinic brief (executed, HTTP 200, id deliv-0049e8bf) returned content whose Executive Summary first line is "Subject: Lead with the decision and its cost, keep evidence tight, and prefer concrete numbers, timelines and owners over explanation of fundamentals. (domain: energy)." GET /api/v1/deliverables/deliv-0049e8bf/export (HTTP 200) prints that line two lines under the TRUE "> **Brief:** A 20-bed clinic in rural Kenya…". Root cause confirmed by reading agentic_core/ai/native/engine.py:137-139 — the _subject label tuple is (User, Problem, Challenge, Objective, Mission, Concept, Topic, Research question, Question, Task / question, Task, Hypothesis, Target role, Current situation, Concern, Subject, Search query); "Brief" is absent, while _CONTENT_LABELS at line 149 DOES contain "Brief". With no match, line 141-143 returns the longest sentence, which is the realm directive from taxonomy.py:40 prepended at deliverables.py:108-111. Reproduced on two further surfaces: POST /api/v1/qep/tajweed/lesson {rule_name:"Idgham"} prints the heading "## Terms most frequent in your request" over "triggers pronunciation · transliteration common · educational assistant · assistant supporting · generate structured · pronunciation guide" (the learner typed only "Idgham"; those terms come from the route's own role line and its "Include:" scaffold, via _content falling through to the whole prompt at line 165); POST /api/v1/qep/adaptation/execute {pattern:"font_size"} prints "The list below is the request's most frequent terms" over "cross-domain adaptation · adjustments expected · pedagogical pattern · adaptation engine · adapted mechanism".
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Add "Brief" (and "Title") to the label tuple in `_subject` at agentic_core/ai/native/engine.py:137 so the brief wins, and make the longest-sentence fallback refuse rather than guess — emit "Subject: (not stated in the request)" and drop the term list when `_content` had to fall back to the whole prompt, rather than labelling scaffolding as the reader's words.

### R1.1 · The Religion hub's "Generate Class Report" button serves floor-composed Qur'an curriculum with no provenance badge, no AI-assisted label and no teacher referral — **STUB** · tier 1

- **severity (assessor):** high
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** A learner or teacher on /religion?tab=qep clicks a button labelled "Generate Class Report" and is shown a block of text beginning "_Acting as: master curriculum designer with expertise in bloom._" followed by "## Learning Objectives", "## Prerequisites", "## Week-by-Week Plan" for Qur'an & Islamic Studies. Two things a reader is told are untrue: that this is a report about their class (the card immediately above it says "No class roster exists on this deployment", and the subject/level/duration are hardcoded in the component, not chosen), and — by the complete absence of any label — that this is curriculum rather than a heading frame. This is not "no model ran" being held against the environment: the route's own payload carries `served_by: native` and `qms_basis: "not assessable — floor-served"`, and the component drops both. Every sibling Religion tool on the same hub renders `floor_note` and `disclaimer`; this one renders neither, so it is not a disclosed shortfall (tier 3) but an unlabelled assertion on the one surface §11 binds hardest.
- **claim:** §11 rule 4 — "Floor-served output is never presented as scholarship or translation … every AI-served surface renders serving provenance (the shared amber 'structured floor — not model analysis' badge)"; §11 rule 5 — "AI content is labelled AI-assisted with provenance — never authoritative; learners are directed to qualified teachers."
- **observed:** apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/LearnTeachModule.tsx:24-32 posts a hardcoded {subject:"Quran & Islamic Studies", level:"Intermediate", duration_weeks:12} to /api/v1/education/curriculum — the generic Education tool, not a Religion route — and lines 97-101 render `report` inside a bare `<pre>`. No provenanceBadge, no floor_note, no disclaimer, no scholar/teacher line, and the response's `ai_provenance` object is never read. The route itself returns no `disclaimer`, no `floor_note` and no `sections_withheld` keys, because it is not one of the four Religion handlers that were given them.
- **evidence:** POST /api/v1/education/curriculum {subject:"Quran & Islamic Studies",level:"Intermediate",duration_weeks:12} executed, HTTP 200. Response keys: curriculum_id, subject, level, duration_weeks, framework, curriculum, ai_provenance, generated_at — no disclaimer, no floor_note. Body opens "_Acting as: master curriculum designer with expertise in bloom._" and fills "## Week-by-Week Plan" with the floor's generic three-bullet plan ("Route richer generation to a model resource…"). Compare POST /api/v1/religion/quran-tafsir, which returns both `floor_note` and a `disclaimer` and whose hub tab renders both.
- **disclosed to the user:** No. Nothing on the panel says the floor served it, that it is AI-assisted, or that it is not reviewed curriculum; the button's own label asserts a class report that does not exist.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). NOT REFUTED — reproduced. Tier 1 stands, but on different ground, because one of the assessor's two pillars is wrong and the task's own rules bar the other. WRONG: "the complete absence of any label" — the rendered <pre> DOES open with "_[Workstation native structured engine — owned, no external dependency]_", which is in the response body. BARRED: the missing badge/disclaimer/teacher line is an omission, and the task states an omission is not an assertion, so the absence of §11's labels cannot by itself be tier 1. What survives, and is enough, are the assertions. (a) The floor publishes "_Acting as: master curriculum designer with expertise in bloom._" over Qur'an & Islamic Studies content — §11 rule 4's exact prohibition, floor output presented as expert work, and the one marker line present boasts ownership rather than correcting it. (b) The button says "Generate Class Report"; A.6 feature 6 (Educator Platform & Class Management) is "not built", the subject/level/duration are hardcoded in the component, no roster exists, and the card two lines above says so — so the label asserts a report about a class that cannot exist. (c) "## Week-by-Week Plan" is a heading asserting a 12-week plan follows, and what follows is the floor's internal engineering instruction to route generation to a model. These are affirmative claims, not blanks, which is why this is tier 1 rather than tier 2; and nothing on the panel states the shortfall, so it is not tier 3. Verdict STUB stands: the surface offers a not-built capability by silently routing to the generic Education tool.
- **refuter's evidence:** apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/LearnTeachModule.tsx:24-32 posts hardcoded {subject:"Quran & Islamic Studies", level:"Intermediate", duration_weeks:12} to /api/v1/education/curriculum and lines 97-101 render `report` in a bare <pre> — no provenanceBadge import, no floor_note, no disclaimer, no scholar/teacher line; the declared response type is only {curriculum?, output?}, so ai_provenance is structurally unread. POST /api/v1/education/curriculum with that exact body executed, HTTP 200; keys = curriculum_id, subject, level, duration_weeks, framework, curriculum, ai_provenance, generated_at — no disclaimer and no floor_note, confirming it is not one of the four religion.py handlers that carry them (contrast POST /api/v1/religion/quran-tafsir, executed, which returns both). The curriculum body opens "_Acting as: master curriculum designer with expertise in bloom._" and its "## Week-by-Week Plan" contains exactly three bullets: "Validate the structured outputs above against the stated objective. / Route richer generation to a model resource (local-first; external only if enabled). / Iterate via the orchestrator's cascade for the stated domain; record outcomes to memory/UEG." Reached at /religion?tab=qep (App.tsx:179 → ReligionHub.tsx:117). ai_provenance does carry served_by "native" and qms_basis "not assessable — floor-served", both dropped by the component.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** In LearnTeachModule.tsx render `provenanceBadge(data.ai_provenance.served_by, …)` plus a fixed line — "Floor-served heading frame for Qur'an study, not reviewed curriculum; study with a qualified teacher" — above the `<pre>`, and rename the button to what it does ("Generate a study-plan frame"). Longer term the Religion hub should call a Religion route that carries `floor_note`/`disclaimer` like its four siblings, rather than the generic Education route.

### R1.2 · The interfaith route is the one Religion tool with no disclaimer, no floor note and no withheld sections — it returns a scholar persona over three faiths' "perspectives" that nothing looked up — **API_ONLY** · tier 1

- **severity (assessor):** high
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The reached form of an API-only surface is its response body, and that body opens "_Acting as: scholar of comparative religion and interfaith dialogue._" and then publishes "## Islam's Perspective on charity", "## Christianity's Perspective on charity", "## Judaism's Perspective on charity", "## Points of Convergence". Those headings assert that each tradition's position is stated below. Nothing was looked up, and — unlike the fatwa, hadith, tafsir and halal routes, each of which withholds its scholarly sections and says so — this payload contains no `disclaimer`, no `floor_note` and no `sections_withheld` key, so no sentence anywhere in it corrects the claim. An omission would be tier 2; a persona claim plus substantive headings with no label is an assertion, and §11 rule 4 names exactly this.
- **claim:** §11 rule 4 — floor-served output is never presented as scholarship, and every AI-served surface renders serving provenance; §11 rule 5 — AI content labelled AI-assisted, never authoritative.
- **observed:** agentic_core/api/religion.py:569-614 `interfaith_dialogue` calls `ai_text(prompt, "religion_interfaith")` and returns {analysis_id, topic, traditions, dialogue_purpose, analysis, ai_provenance, generated_at}. There is no `_model_available()` check, no `_withhold_sections` call, no `floor_note` and no `disclaimer` — the four sibling handlers in the same file (lines 95-126, 176-207, 321-357, 521-555) all have every one of those. The leaked prompt scaffolding ("Structure as: ## Overview ## Islam's Perspective…") is reproduced inside the body of each section, and "## Islam's Perspective on charity" is filled with a commercial-service template ("Offering: what is delivered and the scope boundary. Delivery model… SLAs & assurance"). No UI reaches it: there is no interfaith entry in apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/toolRegistry.ts and no reference anywhere in src except an unrelated KnowledgeHub description string.
- **evidence:** POST /api/v1/religion/interfaith {"topic":"charity"} executed, HTTP 200. Response keys printed: ['analysis_id','topic','traditions','dialogue_purpose','analysis','ai_provenance','generated_at'] — `disclaimer` and `floor_note` absent. Body verified to contain the scholar persona line and the four tradition headings with the service template under the Islam heading.
- **disclosed to the user:** No. The payload carries `ai_provenance.served_by: "native"`, which a caller must interpret; no sentence in the response says no comparative analysis happened.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). NOT REFUTED — reproduced, and worse than filed. Tier 1 stands. An API-only surface's reached form is its response body, and that body makes two assertions nothing behind it supports: a persona claim ("_Acting as: scholar of comparative religion and interfaith dialogue._") and four substantive headings each asserting that a named tradition's position on charity is stated below. §11 rule 4 names exactly this. It cannot fall to tier 2 because a persona plus substantive headings is an assertion, not a blank field; it cannot fall to tier 3 because no sentence in the payload states the shortfall. One correction to the assessor's reasoning, which overstates: "no sentence anywhere in it corrects the claim" is not quite true — most sections do carry "The list below is the request's most frequent terms, not findings about them." But that line qualifies only the term list, says nothing about the floor or about scholarship, and — decisively — the "## Islam's Perspective on charity" section, the one filled with the SLA/delivery-model template, carries no honest line at all. Two further aggravations the assessor missed: the raw prompt scaffold is republished inside each section body, and the route is the only one of five in religion.py with no _model_available guard, so if a model were ever provisioned it would also be the only one that could not refuse a floor answer the way tafsir and halal-review do. Verdict API_ONLY confirmed independently.
- **refuter's evidence:** agentic_core/api/religion.py:570-614 read in full: interfaith_dialogue calls ai_text(prompt, "religion_interfaith") and returns {analysis_id, topic, traditions, dialogue_purpose, analysis, ai_provenance, generated_at}. No _model_available() check, no _withhold_sections call, no floor_note, no disclaimer — the four siblings in the same file (quran-tafsir, hadith, fatwa/dialogue, halal-review) each have all of them; I executed halal-review and quran-tafsir and both returned sections_withheld + floor_note + disclaimer. POST /api/v1/religion/interfaith {"topic":"charity"} executed, HTTP 200; keys printed ['analysis_id','topic','traditions','dialogue_purpose','analysis','ai_provenance','generated_at'] — disclaimer, floor_note and sections_withheld all absent. The body opens "_Acting as: scholar of comparative religion and interfaith dialogue._" then "## Overview", "## Islam's Perspective on charity", "## Christianity's Perspective on charity", "## Judaism's Perspective on charity", "## Points of Convergence". Under "## Islam's Perspective on charity" the floor prints a commercial-services template: "Offering: what is delivered and the scope boundary. / Delivery model: how it is provided (cadence, channel, roles). / Quality measure for charity. / SLAs & assurance: the commitments and how they're governed for the stated domain." The prompt's own scaffold ("Structure as: ## Overview ## Islam's Perspective on charity …") is reproduced verbatim inside every section body. Reachability: grep over apps/workstation-superapp/src returns 3 hits for "interfaith", all in one unrelated string — KnowledgeHub.tsx:99 'Cross-domain synthesis and interfaith dialogue.' No toolRegistry entry, no route.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Give `interfaith_dialogue` the same three lines its four siblings have: gate on `_model_available()`, pass the per-tradition perspective headings and Points of Convergence/Divergence to `_withhold_sections`, and return a `floor_note` plus a `disclaimer` saying nothing was researched and no tradition's position was looked up.

### R1.3 · Written-recall scores a correctly remembered ayah typed without harakat at 50% and reports a madd the learner did not miss — **PARTIAL** · tier 2

- **severity (assessor):** medium
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** A learner who types the Basmala correctly but without diacritics — how Arabic is ordinarily typed — is shown "written similarity 50%" and an amber line "madd (kasra+ya) sequence present in reference, absent in attempt". The 50% is a true normalised Levenshtein ratio and `similarity_basis` names the method, so no figure is fabricated: that keeps it off tier 1. But nothing on the surface says that harakat are counted as characters, or that an answer with every word right scores about half, so the marker reads to the learner as a memorisation gap they do not have. That is a real shortfall the surface does not state, which is tier 2 rather than tier 3.
- **claim:** §11 rule 3 / A.9.1 — assessment is written-recall only, "the learner's typed Arabic against the authoritative text, with its scope stated"; A.6 feature 3 is DELIVERED-and-honest on this basis.
- **observed:** agentic_core/religious_domain/tajwid/coach.py `compare_written_recall` strips whitespace and compares raw strings; `_levenshtein_similarity` does no diacritic or alif-form normalisation, and `_missing_rule_markers` looks for the literal two-character sequences "َا", "ُو", "ِي" and "نْ" — every one of which requires a typed harakat. `markers_basis` describes them as "character sequences", and `similarity_basis` says "normalised Levenshtein distance between the typed attempt and the authoritative text"; neither says the authoritative text is fully diacritised or that the comparison is diacritic-sensitive.
- **evidence:** POST /api/v1/qep/tajweed/analyse with ayah_text = the diacritised 1:1 as served by /api/v1/qep/ayah/1/1 and recited_text = "بسم الله الرحمن الرحيم" (every word correct, no harakat) returned text_similarity 0.5 and missing_rule_markers ["madd (kasra+ya) sequence present in reference, absent in attempt"]. apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/QEPStudio.tsx renders this as "written similarity 50%" plus each marker in amber.
- **disclosed to the user:** Partly. The scope line ("says NOTHING about recitation") and the basis lines are shown; the diacritic sensitivity, which is what produced the 50% and the marker, is not.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). NOT REFUTED — reproduced to the exact figure and the exact marker, and the surface reading is slightly worse than filed. Tier 2 is right. It is not tier 1: 0.5 is a true normalised Levenshtein ratio, similarity_basis names the method, and the marker sentence is literally accurate as a statement about character sequences — no figure is fabricated and nothing is certified. It is not tier 3: nothing anywhere on the surface says harakat are counted as characters, so a learner who reproduced the ayah perfectly is shown "written similarity 50%" and an amber line reading as a memorisation failure they do not have, and that shortfall is invisible. Two corrections, both strengthening it. (a) The assessor records disclosed=Partly on the basis that "the basis lines are shown" — markers_basis, the one string containing the words "character sequences", is NOT rendered at all; QEPStudio.tsx:271 prints only the marker text. So the single qualifier that would let a learner decode the amber line never reaches them. (b) The defect is wider than diacritics: the authoritative text uses U+0671, so even a learner who types every harakat correctly with an ordinary U+0627 alef is penalised three times in this ayah. The remedy is a disclosure (or normalisation with its scope stated), not a change to the figure.
- **refuter's evidence:** Executed: GET /api/v1/qep/ayah/1/1 returns text_arabic of 38 codepoints, fully diacritised, and using U+0671 ALEF WASLA for the three definite articles. POST /api/v1/qep/tajweed/analyse with ayah_text = that exact string and recited_text = the word-perfect undiacritised Basmala (U+0628 U+0633 U+0645 …) returned text_similarity 0.5 and missing_rule_markers ["madd (kasra+ya) sequence present in reference, absent in attempt"], comparable true. Source read: coach.py:87-103 _levenshtein_similarity does no normalisation of any kind (it strips whitespace, then compares codepoint by codepoint); coach.py:105-119 _missing_rule_markers tests for the literal two-codepoint sequences "َا", "ُو", "ِي" and "نْ", each of which requires a typed harakat. Rendering read at QEPStudio.tsx:270-272: "written similarity {Math.round(x*100)}%" with similarity_basis as a title attribute, then each marker as a plain amber <p>, then scope in italics.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Either normalise harakat out of both strings before the comparison and report the consonantal-skeleton similarity separately from a diacritics figure, or — the one-line version — extend `similarity_basis` and `markers_basis` to state "diacritics (harakat) are compared as characters: an attempt typed without them scores lower and will show madd/ghunnah markers as absent".

### R1.4 · The §10 bar on the deliverables path reports modelled, simulated, optimised and ranked as "not measured by this gate" on a path that never attempts any of them — **PARTIAL** · tier 3 *(assessed tier 2)*

- **severity (assessor):** medium
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The reader is shown a chip "§10 bar: 0 measured · 0 attested · 2 screen-only · 16 not measured" and, per criterion, the basis "not measured by this gate". That phrasing is a claim about the instrument, not about the activity, and it invites the reading that modelling and ranking happened and this particular gate did not score them. On the Genesis path the same record says the opposite thing honestly — "not attested: candidates_distinct=1 of 3 — one text modelled 3 times is not 3 candidates modelled" — which names the absence. Nothing fabricates a figure here, so it is not tier 1; but the surface never states that the deliverables path performs no modelling, simulation, optimisation or ranking at all, so it is not a disclosed shortfall either.
- **claim:** §10 — "Every solution is specifically designed · modelled · simulated · optimised · categorised · ranked"; §15.5 — "Modelled · simulated · optimised · ranked before delivery."
- **observed:** agentic_core/api/deliverables.py:241-301 `produce` makes exactly one `_generate` call (or ingests caller content verbatim) and then calls `assure_delivery`. There is no candidate generation, no digital-twin simulation, no scoring and no selection anywhere on the path — the four criteria are structurally unreachable, not merely unmeasured. By contrast agentic_core/api/genesis.py:185-238 `_bar_attestations` builds candidates, simulates them, screens them and then withholds each criterion with a reason that names what did not happen.
- **evidence:** POST /api/v1/deliverables/produce (executed, HTTP 200): `quality_assurance.quality.bar_measured.criteria.modelled/simulated/optimised/ranked` each = {met: null, basis: "not measured by this gate", measured: false, attested: false, source: "none"}. The same journey run gave, for `modelled`, "not attested: candidates_distinct=1 of 3 — one text modelled 3 times is not 3 candidates modelled". apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/Deliverables.tsx:251-256 renders only the summary count and the tooltip "not measured: nothing established it".
- **disclosed to the user:** No. "Not measured by this gate" and "nothing established it" both describe measurement; neither says the deliverables path does not model, simulate, optimise or rank.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). NOT REFUTED as a gap — the deliverables path genuinely performs no modelling, simulation, optimisation or ranking, and Genesis genuinely does it honestly next door. TIER CORRECTED DOWN, 2 → 3, and I want that move on the record. The assessor's tier-2 case rests on "not measured by this gate" implying that modelling happened and went unscored. Two things defeat it. First, that phrase is not on the reader's surface: the page renders only the chip and the legend "not measured: nothing established it", which is a direct statement that no evidence exists for any of the sixteen. Second, in the form where the phrase IS reached — the API body and the stored record — it sits beside source: "none" and attested: false on every one of those twelve criteria, so a consumer is told that neither the gate measured it nor any caller even claimed it. By the tier rules I was given, an omission is not an assertion and "not measured by this gate" asserts no outcome; a shortfall the surface states is tier 3 however large. Nothing here fabricates a figure (which is why it is not tier 1) and nothing is invisible (which is why it is not tier 2) — 0 of 16 with "nothing established it" is the disclosure. What remains is a real and worthwhile improvement — adopt Genesis's wording and name the absence ("this path produces one draft; no candidates were modelled, simulated, optimised or ranked") — but that upgrades a disclosed gap, it does not correct an untruth. Verdict PARTIAL stands.
- **refuter's evidence:** Executed POST /api/v1/deliverables/produce; quality_assurance.quality.bar_measured printed in full: measured 0, met 0, attested 0, not_measured 16, screen_only 2 (compliant, safe), measured_criteria [], attested_criteria [], summary "0 measured · 0 attested · 2 screen-only · 16 not measured". Each of modelled / simulated / optimised / categorised / ranked / best-in-class / innovative / effective / efficient / commercially viable / tested / validated = {met: null, basis: "not measured by this gate", measured: false, attested: false, source: "none"}. The structural claim checks out: deliverables.py:241-301 read in full — one _generate call (or verbatim ingest of caller content), then assure_delivery; no candidate construction, no twin, no scoring, no selection anywhere on the path. The contrast checks out too: genesis.py:185-238 _bar_attestations withholds each criterion with a reason naming the absence ("candidates_distinct=1 of 3 — one text modelled 3 times is not 3 candidates modelled"; "the twin returned one identical output for all N candidates — nothing was simulated apart"). The string is a single default at vbs/quality.py:187 (unmeasured()). What a UI reader sees, read at Deliverables.tsx:251-256: the chip "§10 bar: 0 measured · 0 attested · 2 screen-only · 16 not measured" with the tooltip "§10 — measured: this gate computed it. attested: a caller asserted it and nothing verified the claim. not measured: nothing established it." The per-criterion basis "not measured by this gate" is not rendered on the page at all; it is reached only in the API response and the stored record.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Where the delivery path has no such stage, set the basis to "not performed on this path — producing a deliverable composes one draft; modelling, simulation, optimisation and ranking happen only on a Genesis journey (/genesis)", and add a one-line note under the §10 chip on Deliverables.tsx saying the same, with the link.

### R1.5 · The AI Tools Catalogue card still promises halal "certification guidance" that the route withholds and never produced — **PARTIAL** · tier 2

- **severity (assessor):** medium
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The card a user reads before opening the tool says it delivers "flags, process concerns, certification guidance". The route withholds Halal Status Assessment, Critical Issues and Flagged Ingredients, and emits no certification guidance in any form. A reader is told the tool produces something it never produces — but the result page then names the withheld sections and prints "A halal status comes from an accredited certifying body", so the shortfall is disclosed one click later and this is not a standing untruth about an output. The card itself says nothing about it, which is tier 2, not tier 3.
- **claim:** §11 — halal/Sharia compliance is a live engine, never a certification; §15.10 — launch-ready and honest, stubs never ship. Owner ruling 2026-09-29: halal is assessed by certificate verification, the body clears, never the platform.
- **observed:** apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/toolRegistry.ts:27 reads `form('halal', 'Halal Certification Pre-Assessment', 'Halal pre-assessment of a product and its ingredients: flags, process concerns, certification guidance.')`, and apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/AIToolsCatalogue.tsx:58 renders `t.desc` verbatim. The hub's own `DomainTool description` three lines away in ReligionHub.tsx WAS corrected (its comment records that the blurb "promised three things and the screen showed none of them") — the registry entry, which is the front-door copy, was left as it was. The registry file's header comment at lines 22-24 states the rule it breaks: "The card says what comes out."
- **evidence:** POST /api/v1/religion/halal-review {product_name:"Gelatin gummy", ingredients:["gelatin","glucose syrup"]} executed, HTTP 200: sections_withheld = ["Halal Status Assessment","Critical Issues","Flagged Ingredients"]; floor_note = "…no halal status, critical issues or flagged ingredients are offered…"; the only substantive output is `ingredient_screen` (a 34-term deterministic word list). No certification guidance appears anywhere in the payload.
- **disclosed to the user:** Not on the card. The result page discloses the withheld sections and the screen's basis.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). NOT REFUTED on its core — the front-door card is uncorrected while the hub's own copy three files away was fixed, which is verified text, not inference. But the assessor's evidence contains a flat factual error I have to correct: "No certification guidance appears anywhere in the payload" is FALSE. religion.py:507-510 puts "## Recommended Certifying Bodies (by target market)", "## Steps to Achieve Certification" and "## Market-Specific Requirements" in the prompt UNCONDITIONALLY, and _judging (the withheld set) contains only the three judging sections — so the executed payload does carry both certification headings. What it carries under them is nothing: "## Recommended Certifying Bodies" is filled with "Generate ≥3 distinct variants of the approach. Score each against value, feasibility, risk, and values-fit (halal/beneficence)." — the floor instructing itself — and "## Steps to Achieve Certification" with the generic "Route richer generation to a model resource" triplet. So the defect is not that the promise is withheld; it is that the promise is answered by two headings with the engine's own scaffolding beneath them. Tier 2 survives that correction and I keep it. Not tier 1: the result page states the shortfall where it matters — floor_note names the three withheld sections, says "the notes that remain are a structured frame", and says a halal status comes from an accredited body, which is also the Owner's 2026-09-29 ruling honoured exactly. Not tier 3: the card a user reads BEFORE opening the tool promises certification guidance and says nothing about the limits, and a card is a surface of its own. The remedy is one string at toolRegistry.ts:27.
- **refuter's evidence:** Divergence verified by reading both strings. toolRegistry.ts:27: form('halal', 'Halal Certification Pre-Assessment', 'Halal pre-assessment of a product and its ingredients: flags, process concerns, certification guidance.') — rendered verbatim at AIToolsCatalogue.tsx:58 ({t.desc}). ReligionHub.tsx:207 was corrected: "…it flags; it never clears, and it is not certification. Where the owned model is unavailable some sections are withheld rather than guessed, and the result says which", with a W574 comment at lines 199-205 recording that the old blurb "PROMISED THREE THINGS AND THE SCREEN SHOWED NONE OF THEM". The registry file's own header comment at lines 22-24 states the rule: "The card says what comes out." POST /api/v1/religion/halal-review {product_name:"Gelatin gummy", product_description:"A chewy gummy sweet", ingredients:["gelatin","glucose syrup"]} executed, HTTP 200: sections_withheld ["Halal Status Assessment","Critical Issues","Flagged Ingredients"]; floor_note ends "A halal status comes from an accredited certifying body"; ingredient_screen flags gelatin with the reason "may be porcine or non-zabiha bovine; the source animal and slaughter method decide" and a basis naming a 34-term screen with verdict null.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Change the registry desc at toolRegistry.ts:27 to match the corrected tool copy: "Screens declared ingredients against the terms that commonly require verification. It flags; it never clears, and it is not certification — a halal status comes from an accredited certifying body."

### R1.6 · A QEP roadmap card advertises emotion-driven interface adaptation, the capability A.9.4 refuses outright — **PARTIAL** *(assessed DOC_OVERCLAIM)* · tier 2

- **severity (assessor):** medium
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The card reads "Adaptive UI — Interface adjusts to age and emotion" with an amber "planned" badge. The badge means nothing is claimed to work today, so this is not a fabricated present capability (tier 1). But A.9.4 does not defer emotion inference — it refuses it, for stated reasons (no consent model, the population includes minors, an inferred state presented as known fails the honesty bar). Advertising it as a forward commitment tells a reader the platform intends to infer their emotional state, which is untrue and which the surface does not qualify anywhere. A ratified refusal presented as a roadmap item is an undisclosed misstatement, not a disclosed gap.
- **claim:** A.9.4 — "Emotion inference is not adopted … QEP's adaptive UI adapts to profile and stated preference (the W428 explicit profile), never to inferred affect. Reopening this needs an Owner ruling with a consent model attached." A.6 feature 12 is scoped to "profile and stated preference".
- **observed:** apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/QEPFlagshipFeatures.tsx:33 — `{ id: 'adaptive_ui', name: 'Adaptive UI', … desc: 'Interface adjusts to age and emotion.' }`, rendered on the Religion hub under the heading "Roadmap — modules not yet built (honestly labelled)". The platform elsewhere gets this right: ReligionHub.tsx:44-47 badges the stored tone value as "{emotionalAdjustment} TONE (saved pref.)" with the title "a saved preference — no request from this hub carries it yet", which is the A.9.4-compliant posture. The same file's `swarm` card ("Swarm Learning — AI-coordinated group pacing") advertises as a planned QEP module what A.6 feature 11 says "must not be built as one".
- **evidence:** Read from source; the card is reachable at /religion?tab=qep under the QEPFlagshipFeatures roadmap grid. No qualifier accompanies the desc — eight of the thirteen cards carry an honesty clause in their desc text ("Planned: recitation analysis needs a phonetic model none is provisioned", "no backend exists yet", "no issuing mechanism exists yet"); this one does not.
- **disclosed to the user:** No. The "planned" badge discloses that it does not work; nothing discloses that it is refused and will not be built in this form.
- **refutation: STOOD, verdict CORRECTED DOC_OVERCLAIM → PARTIAL.** NOT REFUTED — the card text is verbatim what was filed and A.9.4 refuses rather than defers the capability. VERDICT CORRECTED, DOC_OVERCLAIM → PARTIAL: DOC_OVERCLAIM means the vision or a canon doc claims more than the system does, and here the vision is the party that is RIGHT — A.9.4 refuses emotion inference and the component advertises it, so the verdict as filed points the finding at the wrong artefact and a register row built on it would send someone to amend the vision. The defect is in the roadmap surface, which is otherwise delivered and largely honest (planned badges on all twelve unbuilt cards, "Not yet built — nothing was run" on selection, and five explicit honesty clauses), with a shortfall. TIER 2 UNCHANGED. Not tier 1: the amber badge and the "nothing was run" line mean no reader relies on a false output or a fabricated figure. Not tier 3: a heading that calls itself "honestly labelled" over a card advertising a ratified refusal does not disclose that it is refused, and the detail panel actively asserts the opposite — "This module is described in the platform plan" is a false claim of intent about sensing a learner's emotional state, which is the one thing A.9.4 says cannot be reopened without a consent model. Two evidence corrections, both widening it: the assessor's count is wrong — FIVE of thirteen cards carry an honesty clause, not eight, so the unqualified set is adaptive_ui, swarm, ar_vr, education, community, analytics, offline and assistant; and swarm ('AI-coordinated group pacing') advertises as a planned QEP module the thing A.6 feature 11 says "must not be built as one", which is the same class in the same array.
- **refuter's evidence:** QEPFlagshipFeatures.tsx:33 read verbatim: { id: 'adaptive_ui', name: 'Adaptive UI', icon: Palette, color: 'text-highlight', desc: 'Interface adjusts to age and emotion.' }. Render block read at lines 60-72: every card except memorization carries an amber span reading 'planned', under the ReligionHub.tsx:122 heading "Roadmap — modules not yet built (honestly labelled)". Detail panel read at lines 100-110: selecting a card shows "{name} Module", the amber line "Not yet built — nothing was run", and the sentence "This module is described in the platform plan but has no backend yet, so no result can be shown — a fabricated one would be worse than none." A.9.4 read in full: emotion inference is "Not adopted", for stated reasons (no consent/retention/opt-out model in the sources, the population includes minors, an inferred state presented as known fails the honesty bar), and "Reopening this needs an Owner ruling with a consent model attached." The compliant posture exists elsewhere, as filed: ReligionHub.tsx:44-47 badges the stored tone value "{emotionalAdjustment} TONE (saved pref.)" titled "a saved preference — no request from this hub carries it yet".
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Change the desc to the ratified scope and say why: "Interface adapts to your stated profile and preferences. Emotion inference is not adopted — an inferred emotional state presented as known fails the honesty bar, and the platform will not do it." Same treatment for the `swarm` card.

### R1.7 · A leaderboard button promises a launch with "QEP Season 2", a release that exists nowhere in the system or the plan — **PARTIAL** · tier 2

- **severity (assessor):** low
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The tournament cards are correctly badged PLANNED with zero participants, so the absence of competitions is disclosed and that part is tier 3. The toast goes further than disclosure: "Global leaderboard launching with QEP Season 2" asserts a named future release that does not exist in any plan, store, route or document in the repository. A reader is told the feature is scheduled. That is a statement of fact the system cannot support, and no part of the surface qualifies it — tier 2 rather than tier 1 only because the card beside it already says PLANNED and 0 participants, so no one is told the leaderboard works now.
- **claim:** A.6 feature 4 — "Competitions, leaderboards and badges do not exist"; A.6 feature 9's blessed posture is "rendered as planned, and the buttons say so on click". §15.6 honesty over polish; §15.10 stubs never ship.
- **observed:** apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/domains/QEPReligionHub.tsx:234 — `onClick={() => toast('Global leaderboard launching with QEP Season 2')}`. A repository-wide grep for "QEP Season" finds this line, one archived file (_archive/frontend-pages/ABTesting.tsx, not shipped) and nothing else: no plan item, no store, no document defines a Season. The same file's AR/VR buttons toast "coming in Phase 4" (lines 215-216) and no Phase 4 commitment covering AR/VR exists in docs/FABLE_DELIVERY_PROMPT.md either.
- **evidence:** Read from source; reachable at /qep → Competitions tab → View Leaderboard, and /qep → AR/VR Lab → both buttons. Verified by execution that no tournament or leaderboard route exists: the openapi document has no leaderboard, competition or tournament path among its 496 paths.
- **disclosed to the user:** Partly. The PLANNED badge and the 0-participant count are honest; the toast adds a schedule nothing backs.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). NOT REFUTED — the toast string, the zero-participant PLANNED cards and the absence of any backing route are all verified first-hand. Tier 2 is right. Not tier 1: the card beside the button says PLANNED with 0 participants, so no one is told the leaderboard works now and no figure is fabricated. Not tier 3: "launching with QEP Season 2" asserts a named future release, and nothing in the repository — no plan item, no store, no route among 496, no document — defines a Season; the PLANNED badge discloses that the feature is absent, which is a different statement from the schedule the toast adds, and nothing on the surface qualifies that schedule. One note for the register rather than a correction: the grep shows this exact string was recorded as an unfixed residual in a prior ledger edition, so it has survived at least one round; I did not read that document beyond the single grep line and every fact above is independently verified. The AR/VR half of the finding is weaker than the leaderboard half and I would not carry it — "coming in Phase 4" sits directly beneath a heading that names the module planned and states no WebXR code exists, so that part is disclosed.
- **refuter's evidence:** QEPReligionHub.tsx:234 read verbatim: <Button onClick={() => toast('Global leaderboard launching with QEP Season 2')} … >View Leaderboard</Button>, inside TournamentCard, which is instantiated three times at lines 200-202 — each with players={0} and status="PLANNED". Reachable at /qep and /qep-religion (App.tsx:185-186). Repository-wide grep for "QEP Season" across tsx/ts/py/md/json returns exactly three hits: this line, _archive/frontend-pages/ABTesting.tsx:62 (not shipped), and one line of a prior ledger document. Verified by execution that nothing backs it: GET /openapi.json has 496 paths and the substrings leaderboard, competition, tournament, season and badge each match ZERO of them. The AR/VR toasts at lines 215-216 ("coming in Phase 4") sit under a card heading that does disclose the state: "Immersive Lab — planned (Phase 4)" with "no WebXR code exists yet, so nothing here claims readiness".
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Replace both toast strings with what is true and nothing more: "No tournament or leaderboard backend exists on this deployment." and "No WebXR code exists on this deployment." — dropping the invented release names.

### R1.8 · §11's "continuously live" compliance is one entity per beat behind an off-by-default toggle, over screens that can refuse but never clear — and every one of those limits is stated on the surface — **PARTIAL** · tier 3

- **severity (assessor):** medium
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** This is the largest gap in my region and it is tier 3 precisely because the system says so, everywhere a reader looks. /compliance tells the user "NOTHING here assessed this subject. A keyword screen can refuse a subject; it cannot clear one", lists the eight modules that really call the screen each with its file path, and names its own non-consumers ("the Synthesis studio does not call this screen at all"). The heartbeat toggle copy says "Re-screens ONE living VSB per beat in round-robin … a given entity is re-screened only about once every N beats", and the status reports auto_compliance false, last_compliance null. A shortfall the surface itself states is tier 3 however large it is; nothing here is fabricated and nothing is invisible.
- **claim:** §11 — "Continuously monitored and evaluated live compliance to legal, regulatory, international, and industry best-practice standards … Compliance engines (Halal/Sharia · UK-Legal/London · Regulatory · EHS · Ethical · Constitutional/gaas) are integrated into every synthesis/generative/operational workflow, not bolted on."
- **observed:** All six frameworks exist and are integrated into the delivery gate (agentic_core/vbs/quality.py calls `screen_compliance`, so every deliverable, tafsir, hadith, fatwa, halal and curriculum run carries a verdict). But: every framework is a keyword or vocabulary screen that cannot issue a pass, so on real subjects the overall is always "review" with `compliant: null` and `assessed_by: []`; "every workflow" is ten importing modules, not every workflow; and the continuous half (`_compliance_beat` in agentic_core/organism/heartbeat.py:930, re-screening one VSB per beat round-robin) is opt-in and off.
- **evidence:** POST /api/v1/compliance/check {"subject":"A halal-certified chicken supply chain for UK retail"} executed: overall "review", compliant null, assessed_by [], coverage_gaps [constitutional, ehs, ethical, regulatory, uk_legal], vocabulary_only [sharia_halal], with the sharia row reading "that is the subject's own claim, not a certification and not an assessment". GET /api/v1/heartbeat/status executed: auto_compliance false, last_compliance null, beats 15. GET /api/v1/vbs/qms/defects: gates_run 0, defects_total 0, with the rate basis naming the shared store. GET /api/v1/vbs/qms/document-control: audit_integrity 1.0, verified as a real recomputation in agentic_core/vbs/dcms.py:88-98 (SHA3-512 recomputed over stored content, 0.0 with no verifiable history) — not a constant.
- **disclosed to the user:** Yes, thoroughly — at the compliance screen, on each verdict row, in the delivery record's `basis`, and in the heartbeat toggle copy.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). NOT REFUTED, and tier 3 is right for the reason the assessor gives: this is the largest gap in the region and the system states it everywhere a reader looks — at the screen, in each verdict row, in the delivery record's basis, and in the heartbeat toggle copy and status. Nothing is fabricated, so not tier 1; nothing is invisible, so not tier 2; and the tier rule is explicit that a shortfall the surface states is tier 3 however large. Two small evidence drifts, neither material: GET /api/v1/vbs/qms/defects now reports defects_total 1 and open 1, not 0 — the one row is a what-if gate defect which the rate_basis explicitly excludes from the rate, so the disclosure covers it; and beats reads 27 rather than 15 as the process keeps running. One residual I would raise separately rather than against this finding: that same payload prints non_conformance_rate 0.0 alongside gates_run 0, which is a 0/0 rendered as a clean zero rate — it stays tier 3 only because gates_run sits in the same object and rate_basis names the formula, but a figure that looks like a perfect record computed from no data is worth a basis string of its own.
- **refuter's evidence:** All re-executed. POST /api/v1/compliance/check {"subject":"A halal-certified chicken supply chain for UK retail"} → overall "review", compliant null, assessed_by [], coverage_gaps [constitutional, ehs, ethical, regulatory, uk_legal], vocabulary_only [sharia_halal], and a basis that says it for itself: "A screen can refuse and can escalate; it cannot clear — so only a row with coverage 'engine' can carry a pass, and coverage_gaps names every area nothing here assessed… NOTHING here assessed this subject, so the overall is 'review' and no part of it may be reported as compliant." GET /api/v1/heartbeat/status → auto_compliance false, last_compliance null, beats 27. heartbeat.py:930-947 read: _compliance_beat re-screens the least-recently-screened living VSB, one per beat, round-robin. "Every workflow" measured: grep for screen_compliance returns 10 importing modules (api/compliance, api/genesis, api/marketplace, api/swarm, api/v138/ceo, economy/charity, governance/adaptive_profiles, governance/industry_adaptive, organism/heartbeat, vbs/quality) — and because vbs/quality.py calls it, every gated delivery does carry a verdict. UI disclosure verified at ComplianceChecker.tsx:123: "NOTHING here assessed this subject. A keyword screen can refuse a subject; it cannot clear one." GET /api/v1/vbs/qms/document-control → audit_integrity 1.0, and dcms.py:88-98 recomputes SHA3-512 over stored content and returns 0.0 with no verifiable history, so it is a recomputation and not a constant.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Nothing to correct in what is said. The engineering gap is that no framework has an instrument that can clear a subject; the halal path already shows the shape of the answer (certificate verification, where an accredited body clears and the platform only verifies), and extending that pattern to UK-legal and regulatory is what would move any of these rows off "review". Turning auto_compliance on by default would make §11's "continuously" true for living VSBs.

### R1.9 · The faith-content constitution's rules 1 to 3 hold under execution: sourced Arabic, refused nonexistent ayaat, a 503 on translation, and recall that scores text and says so — **DELIVERED**

- **severity (assessor):** none
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** Verdict DELIVERED, so no tier. Verified by execution rather than by reading a claim: each rule was probed with an input designed to make it fail, and each held.
- **claim:** §11 rules 1-3 and A.9.1-A.9.3 — Qur'an Arabic never AI-generated and labelled at source; nonexistent ayaat refused against the real 114-surah table; a truncated range disclosed as the range covered; recitation never scored, assessment written-recall only with its scope stated; translation of sacred text refuses rather than serving a template.
- **observed:** GET /api/v1/qep/ayah/2/255 returns the diacritised ayah with source "alquran.cloud", plus `basmala_separated: false` and a stated basis ("this ayah carries no prepended Basmala in this edition"). GET /api/v1/qep/ayah/1/9 refuses with HTTP 422 "surah 1 has 7 ayaat — ayah 9 does not exist". POST /api/v1/qep/translation/translate refuses with HTTP 503 before any model call, and GET /api/v1/qep/translation/status computes the same availability so the chip and the click agree. POST /api/v1/qep/tajweed/analyse with transliterated input returns `comparable: false` rather than scoring it, and every payload carries "This says NOTHING about recitation, pronunciation, makharij, or sifat". POST /api/v1/religion/quran-tafsir returns the sourced Arabic with its source label, withholds Transliteration and Translation by name, and carries both a floor_note and a disclaimer — the A.11 defect where arabic_text, arabic_source and reference never reached the screen is closed: ReligionHub.tsx:176-187 renders all of them, with an explicit amber line when the source is unreachable. The /qep recitation panel shows no score, derives every label from the retrieval response, and keeps no fallback copy of scripture. GET /api/v1/qep/compliance/audit returns `compliant: null` with two controls reporting not_checked and the reason "a pass was previously asserted"; /api/qep/analytics/overview returns `measured: false`; the Scholar Governance Board says no scholars are verified; /api/v1/qep/adaptation/execute records a blueprint and states "nothing was installed or activated anywhere".
- **evidence:** All of the above executed against http://127.0.0.1:8031 and the status codes and bodies read. Supporting source: agentic_core/religious_domain/tajwid/coach.py (the W439 rewrite, which refuses non-Arabic and makes no pronunciation claim), agentic_core/api/religion.py:366-433 (`_model_available` + `_withhold_sections`), apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/api.ts:69-96 (the provenance badge's third state, "provenance not recorded", instead of defaulting to a positive floor claim).
- **disclosed to the user:** Yes — the disclosure is the delivery here.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). NOT REFUTED — I re-ran every probe myself rather than accepting the list, and each rule held against an input designed to break it. §11 rules 1-3 and A.9.1-A.9.3 are delivered: Arabic is sourced and labelled at the point of display, nonexistent ayaat are refused with the real count in the message, a truncated range is never claimed whole, translation refuses before any model call rather than serving a template, and written recall refuses what it cannot compare and states its scope in every payload. Verdict DELIVERED stands and tier 0 is correct, since the task reserves tier 0 for DELIVERED. One residual worth a register row, which does not overturn the verdict: the API returns range_note null even on a truncated range, so the dedicated amber "Range:" line at ReligionHub.tsx:183 is dead code and the truncation reaches a user only because the reference field reads "Surah 1:5-7" — rule 2 is satisfied (the covered range is shown, the whole range never claimed) but a user who typed 5-20 has to notice the reference changed under them rather than being told. I also note one place this finding's own §11 rule 4-5 evidence does NOT generalise, which is exactly the hole findings 1 and 2 occupy: the four religion.py handlers that withhold and disclose are the four this finding cites, and the interfaith handler and the education-curriculum route reached from the same hub have none of that machinery.
- **refuter's evidence:** Independently re-probed, each with an input chosen to make the rule fail. GET /api/v1/qep/ayah/2/255 → HTTP 200, 427 codepoints of diacritised Arabic, source alquran.cloud, basmala_separated false with basis "this ayah carries no prepended Basmala in this edition". Refusals against the real ayah table: /qep/ayah/1/9 → 422 "surah 1 has 7 ayaat — ayah 9 does not exist"; /qep/ayah/2/287 → 422 "surah 2 has 286 ayaat"; /qep/ayah/115/1 → 400 "Surah number must be 1-114." Translation: POST /qep/translation/translate {text:"bismillah",target_language:"English"} → 503 "the deterministic native floor cannot translate and its output will not be presented as a translation", and GET /qep/translation/status computes translation_available false from the same availability, so chip and click agree. Recitation never scored: POST /qep/tajweed/analyse with transliterated Latin input → comparable false, "nothing is scored that cannot be compared"; every payload carries "This says NOTHING about recitation, pronunciation, makharij, or sifat" plus a top-level disclaimer naming a qualified teacher. Tafsir: POST /religion/quran-tafsir {surah:1,ayah_start:1,ayah_end:1} → arabic_text present, arabic_source "alquran.cloud — authentic sourced text, never AI-generated", reference "Surah 1:1", sections_withheld [Transliteration, Translation], plus floor_note and disclaimer; ReligionHub.tsx:174-188 renders reference, arabic_text, arabic_source, floor_note and the withheld list, with an amber fallback "The authoritative Arabic source is unreachable — the Arabic is never AI-generated, so none is shown." Truncated range: asking 1:5-20 returns reference "Surah 1:5-7" and ayah_end 7. Honest emptiness: GET /qep/compliance/audit → compliant null with controls not_checked and the reason "a pass was previously asserted"; GET /api/qep/analytics/overview → measured false with "This endpoint previously returned hardcoded figures that no measurement produced"; POST /qep/adaptation/execute → status_note "a blueprint was generated and recorded — nothing was installed or activated anywhere; 'executed'/'active' were the old overclaims". provenanceBadge's third state verified at api.ts:85-88 ("provenance not recorded" rather than defaulting to a positive floor claim). Scholar Governance Board text verified at LearnTeachModule.tsx:120-125.
- **residual lead (assessor's note on a DELIVERED entry — a lead, not a defect):** None needed. The three unlabelled surfaces in findings 2, 3 and 4 are the exceptions to this standard, not evidence against it; each is one or two lines away from the posture the rest of the Religion domain already holds.
