## R3 — §5 + §17.3 + §17.4 — the living organisation, the living business system layers, the three integration modes

**Assessor's region summary:** R3 (§5 living organisation · §17.3 living business system layers · §17.4 the three integration modes), measured against HEAD 6e63e762 by executing the routes on the live backend at 127.0.0.1:8031 and reading the handlers and the superapp source (I could not drive a browser, so every UI statement below is reasoned from the component source and the route it calls, and I say which component and line).  The governance spine of this region is real and I verified it by driving it, not by reading claims about it. Arms-length Change Control holds a HIGH change back: submit → review (approved by the organism-health rule, with the review_result stating that the rule and not the serving resource decided, and that the floor's prose had no bearing) → implement refused 409 → Board ratification → ratified, every step UEG-logged, and `?force` deliberately cannot pass the ratification gate. Mode 3 review gates genuinely refuse: with build and commercialise pending, both the board pack and a cascade scoped to the entity came back 409 naming the gate and the way out, and the shared guard has 13 call sites across the ship, the body generators, evolve, orchestrate and contract delivery. The on-demand Board Pack is assembled fresh and is the most honest artefact I read anywhere in this region — it marks its strategic layer `holds: "placeholder"`, says the action-plan layer holds a roster and not an action plan, and refuses to show the platform's standing values line where the entity has declared none. §17.3's cadence now exists and is heartbeat-driven with per-refresh provenance ("DERIVED from the plan's own stored state by this module - no model composed it"), which is MORE than §17.3's own status paragraph claims ("the Strategic and Action-Plan cadence layers are MISSING") — that paragraph is stale and under-states the system. /board/chief/model is exemplary: it decides twin-versus-role from the Owner's lived record, refuses to let the platform's standing canon stand in for it, and the Board page renders the two cases through different branches.  The gap is concentrated in what the surfaces SAY about content the deterministic floor produced. Five tier-1 defects: the AI CEO prints the floor's three fixed bullets under headings naming the founder's "Living plan" and "Business plan for this scope" while the grounding counts zero of each, and the detector built for exactly this checks one of the three sections (`_GROUNDED_SECTIONS = {"directives": "board directives"}`); the living Roadmap names a Q4 2026 phase current and its objective the next milestone while an incomplete Q1 2026 phase sits last, because phases are ordered by the order objectives were typed; the cascade's governance verdict carries "The delivery's content was NOT screened" and `content_screened: true` in one object, and the Swarm tab shows the false half as the chip's tooltip; the establish stream tells a founder their Board is "chaired by the owner's Chief twin" when /board/chief/model denies any twin exists and /twin/models holds zero; and /organism/cadence republishes a stale beat under a sentence saying it is "what the most recent heartbeat managed" (14:45:21Z, while /heartbeat/status read 14:53:22Z at the same moment). Of the three integration modes, Mode 1 and Mode 3 are built, Mode 2 is absent and says so.  I HIT THE TEN-FINDING CAP AND TEN IS NOT THE SIZE OF THE GAP. Left unreported for space, each verified: the BTO's proposed Products & Services catalogue items are the floor's generic "## Next steps" bullets, one truncated mid-sentence, filed as proposed catalogue entries; `development_applied` returns six run ids for six tier pairs with no basis field saying what "applied" means (the stored action falls back to the whole appraisal text when no Development Action section exists); a board directive engages 1 of the 7 specialist Directors and is stamped `status: "resolved"`; §5's Board-tier deliverable "Action Planning with timelines, resourced tasks, and project management" has no structure anywhere at the Board tier (only the plan's own objectives carry a timeline); POST /business-plan/generate cannot compose the Executive Summary · Concept · Vision at all on the floor and writes nothing, which §15 principle 9 names as in-house-generable; and the QMS gate does not run on floor-served content, so `qms_gate_passed` is null on every cascade — disclosed, but it means the §10 bar is unmeasured on every delivery this environment produces.  Test artefacts I created on the live instance, so a later reader is not misled by them: VSB vsb-9a371566c3 "AquaTrust"; change record cca-8928c69590 (approved, ratified, NOT implemented); board directive dir-06ecf221; three workstation business-plan objectives obj-bc07c4a8, obj-cd78bf1a and the Q1 2026 permit objective added to prove the roadmap ordering defect; two org-cascade runs (e7492adf66, eb710185cb). The review gates I set on vsb-9a371566c3 were cleared again.

### R3.0 · The AI CEO prints the deterministic floor's three generic bullets as the founder's "Living plan" and "Business plan", and the ungrounded-section detector only checks one of the three sections — **PARTIAL** · tier 1

- **severity (assessor):** high
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The answer a reader is shown contains the literal lines "## Living plan\n- Validate the structured outputs above against the stated objective.\n- Route richer generation to a model resource (local-first; external only if enabled).\n- Iterate via the orchestrator's cascade for the stated domain; record outcomes to memory/UEG." and then repeats the same three bullets under "## Business plan for this scope" — while the living plan held 0 objectives and the business plan's executive_summary, concept, vision and mission were all empty strings. These are ASSERTIONS about the founder's own records, not omissions, so this is not tier 2's blank field; and they are not disclosed, because the one instrument built to disclose exactly this (`grounding_conflicts`, W574) flagged only "board directives" — so the page prints one amber "3 unrecorded board directives" chip and nothing at all beside the two other fabricated sections, which a reader then reads as a recitation of their plan. "No model ran" is not the complaint: the floor serving is the environment, and the floor is labelled; the defect is that the floor's own boilerplate is served under headings naming the founder's records with no conflict chip.
- **claim:** §5: "The Chief owns the living Business Plan … and the Chief delivers it via Strategy and a living Roadmap"; the AI CEO "receives the action plan" and the chat's own greeting promises "I answer from the record, and every answer says who served it."
- **observed:** POST /api/v138/ceo/chat (scope workstation) returned an answer with three bullet sections — "Board directives", "Living plan", "Business plan for this scope" — all three carrying the identical three floor bullets, and a final event `grounding: {directives: 0, objectives: 0, plan_score: 0.3, debate_entries: 0}` with `grounding_conflicts` holding exactly ONE entry, for "board directives". The cause is a single-key map: `_GROUNDED_SECTIONS = {"directives": "board directives"}`. `facts` already carries `objectives`, so the "business plan for this scope" heading is checkable today and is not checked; the "living plan" heading has no fact key at all (`plan_score` is the canon's pillar score, not a count of plan content).
- **evidence:** C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/api/v138/ceo.py:352 `_GROUNDED_SECTIONS = {"directives": "board directives"}`; detector at ceo.py:355-382; grounding builder at ceo.py:406-427 (sets facts["objectives"]); rendered at C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/CEOChat.tsx:292-302 (the chip row renders only what `grounding_conflicts` returns). Reached at /ceo (LivingOrganisationHub default tab).
- **disclosed to the user:** Partially — one of three fabricated sections is flagged; the other two are shown with no mark.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Reproduced, and the defect is WIDER than the assessor stated — so tier 1 stands and the finding should not be softened. The detector is not merely single-key: it is gated on the count being 0 or None, so on any scope that HAS records it does not run at all. In my run two real board directives and three real objectives existed, grounding_conflicts came back EMPTY, and the answer still printed the floor's three generic bullets under '## Board directives' instead of either of the two recorded directives, and under '## Living plan' and '## Business plan for this scope' while those records hold three objectives and four empty fields. The page therefore shows a reader a grey chip reading 'grounded in 2 directives · 3 objectives' over text that recites none of them, with no amber chip anywhere — the one instrument built to catch exactly this (W574) is structurally unable to fire on a populated scope. Tier 1 rather than 2 because these are assertions about the content of the founder's own stored records, not blank fields: '## Business plan for this scope' followed by bullets is a claim about what that plan says. Tier 1 rather than 3 because nothing on the surface qualifies it — the floor IS labelled, which is honest provenance, but provenance does not disclose that the headings name records whose content is absent.
- **refuter's evidence:** Executed POST /api/v138/ceo/chat {scope: workstation}. Final event: grounding {directives: 2, objectives: 3, plan_score: 0.3, debate_entries: 0}, grounding_conflicts []. The streamed answer carried '## Board directives' followed by the floor's three fixed bullets ('Validate the structured outputs above against the stated objective.' / 'Route richer generation to a model resource…' / 'Iterate via the orchestrator's cascade…'), then '## Living plan' with the SAME three bullets, then '## Business plan for this scope' with the SAME three bullets. GET /api/v1/business-plan?scope=workstation holds executive_summary '', concept '', vision '', mission '' and three real objectives (obj-bc07c4a8 'Commission the Birmingham filtration pilot', obj-cd78bf1a, obj-9bd0623f) — none of which appear in the answer. Code: agentic_core/api/v138/ceo.py:352 _GROUNDED_SECTIONS = {"directives": "board directives"}; detector at :355-382 skips with `if facts.get(fact_key) not in (0, None): continue`. Render: apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/CEOChat.tsx:292-302 (chip row maps grounding_conflicts only) and :291 (the 'grounded in N directives · N objectives' chip).
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Add the two checkable headings to `_GROUNDED_SECTIONS` — `{"directives": "board directives", "objectives": "business plan for this scope"}` — and add a fact key counting the living plan's own content so "living plan" can be checked too; a heading with no fact key must not be emitted by the prompt scaffold.

### R3.1 · The living Roadmap's "current phase" and "next milestone" are ordered by the order objectives were typed in, not by time, so it names a 2026 Q4 phase current while an incomplete Q1 2026 phase sits last — **PARTIAL** · tier 1

- **severity (assessor):** high
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The surface shows the founder `"current_phase": "Q4 2026"` and `"next_milestone": {"phase": "Q4 2026", "title": "Commission the Birmingham filtration pilot"}` while the same response's phase list reads `["Q4 2026", "next review", "Q1 2026"]` with the Q1 2026 objective at 0% and status planned. "Next milestone" is a definite claim about which objective comes next in TIME — that is the word the vision and the field names use — and the claim is wrong. It is not tier 2, because the shortfall is not a missing field but a specific wrong answer; it is not tier 3, because the only note on the surface is "Living roadmap derived from the plan's objectives — it updates as objectives progress", which never says the phases are in entry order.
- **claim:** §5: the Chief "delivers it via Strategy and a living Roadmap: a time-phased view of the objectives (phases · per-phase progress · current phase · next milestone) that updates as the plan progresses".
- **observed:** Three objectives were added in the order Q4 2026, "next review" (written by the Chief-instruct fallback), Q1 2026. GET /api/v1/business-plan/roadmap then returned phase order ['Q4 2026', 'next review', 'Q1 2026'], current_phase 'Q4 2026', next_milestone the Q4 objective. `_roadmap` builds an `order` list by first appearance of the free-text `timeline` string and takes `current = next(p for p in phase_list if not p["complete"])` — the first incomplete phase in ENTRY order. Nothing parses the timeline, so a placeholder like "next review" also becomes a roadmap phase. The per-phase progress and overall progress arithmetic are correct and do update; the ordering is the defect.
- **evidence:** C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/api/business_plan.py:171-213 (`order.append(tl)` at :183, `current = next((p for p in phase_list if not p["complete"]), None)` at :199); rendered at C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/enterprise/BusinessPlan.tsx:204 ("overall {pct}% · current: {current_phase}") and :218 ("Next milestone: …").
- **disclosed to the user:** No — the note says the roadmap is derived from the objectives and updates; it does not say the phase order is the order they were entered.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Reproduced exactly, and the defect is reachable without any contrived input: the 'next review' phase was written by the platform's OWN chief_instruct fallback (plan objective obj-cd78bf1a carries source 'chief_instruct_fallback', timeline 'next review'), so the platform itself injects a non-temporal placeholder into what it calls a time-phased roadmap. Tier 1 rather than 2 because 'current_phase' and 'next_milestone' are not blank fields — they are specific answers to a question about time, and the answers are wrong: the founder is shown 'current: Q4 2026' and 'Next milestone: Commission the Birmingham filtration pilot (Q4 2026)' while the same response carries an incomplete Q1 2026 phase at 0% sitting last. Tier 1 rather than 3 because the only note on the surface says the roadmap is derived from the objectives and updates as they progress; nothing says the phases are in the order the objectives were entered, and the highlighted card compounds it by visually marking the wrong phase as current.
- **refuter's evidence:** Executed GET /api/v1/business-plan/roadmap?scope=workstation: phases in order [{'timeline':'Q4 2026',...},{'timeline':'next review',...},{'timeline':'Q1 2026','progress_pct':0,'complete':false,'objectives':[{'title':'Secure the Q1 water-quality permit','status':'planned'}]}], current_phase 'Q4 2026', next_milestone {'phase':'Q4 2026','title':'Commission the Birmingham filtration pilot','progress_pct':0}, note 'Living roadmap derived from the plan's objectives — it updates as objectives progress.' Code at C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/api/business_plan.py:176-199: `order.append(tl)` on first appearance of the raw `timeline` string (:183) and `current = next((p for p in phase_list if not p["complete"]), None)` (:199) — no parse of the timeline anywhere. Render at apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/enterprise/BusinessPlan.tsx:204 ('overall {overall_progress_pct}% · current: {current_phase}'), :218 ('Next milestone: {title} ({phase})'), and :208 which highlights the phase card matching current_phase.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Either sort `order` by a parsed date and refuse to phase an unparseable timeline (park those under an explicit "Unscheduled" phase excluded from current/next), or — if free text must stay — rename the fields to `first_incomplete_phase`/`first_incomplete_objective` and state on the surface that phases are listed in the order objectives were added.

### R3.2 · The org cascade's governance verdict says both "The delivery's content was NOT screened" and content_screened: true, and the Swarm tab shows the false half as the chip's tooltip — **PARTIAL** · tier 1

- **severity (assessor):** medium
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** One response object carries `"scope": "intent + domain only — this gate screened the request's intent label against the prohibited list and its own constant attestation sentence. The delivery's content was NOT screened, so this verdict is not a judgement on what was produced."` beside `"screened": "intent + domain + the delivered content of every tier"`, `"content_screened": true`, `"content_chars": 38253` and 15 named tiers. The `scope` sentence is false for this emitter — the content WAS screened — and it is the string the UI puts in the chip's tooltip, so a reader who hovers is told the opposite of what happened. It is an assertion, not an omission, so tier 1 rather than 2; and it is not a disclosed shortfall, because the two fields contradict each other and nothing tells the reader which to believe.
- **claim:** §5: "Change Control (arms-length Agency) — governs all change at arm's length from operations"; §17.5: "mandatory GaaS gate on every output".
- **observed:** POST /api/v1/swarm/cascade returned governance = {status: allowed, scope: "…The delivery's content was NOT screened…", screened: "intent + domain + the delivered content of every tier", content_screened: true, content_chars: 38253, tiers_screened: [15 tiers], content_compliant: true}. W505 genuinely closed the gap W494 had labelled — the post gate now reads all 38,253 characters — but the fix overrode `screened` and `content_screened` and left `scope` at the shared `INTENT_GATE_SCOPE` constant whose whole purpose is to say content was not read.
- **evidence:** C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/gaas/v5/__init__.py:24-26 (INTENT_GATE_SCOPE) and :33 (`intent_gate_result` seeds scope + content_screened: False); C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/api/swarm.py:1002-1008 (the override that omits `scope`); rendered at C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/organism/SwarmIntelligence.tsx:290-295 — `title={cascade.governance.scope}`, and the " · content not screened" suffix is correctly suppressed, so the chip and its own tooltip disagree.
- **disclosed to the user:** No — the contradiction is the disclosure failure; a hover tells the reader the content was not screened.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Reproduced exactly by execution. One response object carries two statements about the same act that cannot both be true: `scope` says the delivery's content was NOT screened, while `content_screened: true`, `content_chars: 35123` and fifteen named tiers say it was. The `scope` sentence is the false one for this emitter, and it is precisely the string the UI hands to the chip's `title`, so the only extra information a reader can get by hovering the governance chip is the opposite of what happened. Tier 1 rather than 2 because this is an assertion, not an omission — a sentence stating that content was not screened, shown over a gate that read 35,123 characters. Tier 1 rather than 3 because a self-contradiction is not a disclosed shortfall: nothing in the response or on the page tells the reader which of the two fields to believe, and the suffix mechanism that would have warned them is (correctly, for this emitter) switched off, leaving the false sentence as the only narrative.
- **refuter's evidence:** Executed POST /api/v1/swarm/cascade {mission, domain: engineering}. governance returned: status 'allowed'; scope 'intent + domain only — this gate screened the request's intent label against the prohibited list and its own constant attestation sentence. The delivery's content was NOT screened, so this verdict is not a judgement on what was produced.'; screened 'intent + domain + the delivered content of every tier'; content_screened true; content_chars 35123; tiers_screened 15 named tiers (Chief … Build-to-Order); content_compliant true. Code: agentic_core/gaas/v5/__init__.py:24-26 defines INTENT_GATE_SCOPE and :31-35 seeds scope + content_screened False; agentic_core/api/swarm.py:1002-1013 overrides screened/content_screened/content_chars/tiers_screened and never replaces `scope`. Render: apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/organism/SwarmIntelligence.tsx:290-295 — `title={cascade.governance.scope}` on the chip, with the ' · content not screened' suffix correctly suppressed by `content_screened === false`.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Give `intent_gate_result` a `scope` argument (or add a `content_gate_result` sibling) so an emitter that screens the content replaces the sentence rather than appending a contradicting field, and make the SwarmIntelligence tooltip read `governance.content_screen_basis` when `content_screened` is true.

The board actually attached to the entity is honest — the board pack's own chief title reads "…no twin model is trained; the Chief reports whether it is a modelled twin or a role" — so only the stream's sentence is wrong.

The finding survives on stronger ground: the writer twenty lines below the false sentence, in the same function, stores 'no twin model is trained', and /api/v1/twin/models holds zero models. Tier 1 rather than 2 because 'the owner's Chief twin' asserts a capability the platform does not have, and does so in the one line a founder reads at the end of establishment; tier 1 rather than 3 because every other Chief surface in this region was corrected to say 'no twin model is trained' — the board roster title, the seeded plan string, SwarmIntelligence.tsx:360, the Board page header — which makes this a surviving second writer rather than a stated gap.

(vsb.py:2089-2090), and its own comment at :2345-2347 reads 'the Owner's standing charter, not a trained twin — W492/FU-188'. ### R3.4 · /api/v1/organism/cadence reports a stale beat as "what the most recent heartbeat managed", because last_cadence is only written on a beat that refreshed or failed — **PARTIAL** · tier 1

- **severity (assessor):** medium
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The response asserts `"last_beat": {"ran": true, "at": "2026-10-05T14:45:21Z", …}` under `"last_beat_basis": "`layers` is the plan's own record of when each layer last refreshed; `last_beat` is what the most recent heartbeat managed."` At the moment of the read, GET /api/v1/heartbeat/status reported `beats: 10, last_beat: "2026-10-05T14:53:22Z"` — eight minutes and roughly eight beats later. So the named timestamp is not the most recent heartbeat, and the sentence saying it is, is untrue. That is an assertion, not an omission, so not tier 2; and it is not tier 3 because the handler's own reading rule ("A layer that is DUE while last_beat.could_not_run is set means the cadence is not firing") instructs the reader to draw a conclusion from a field that can be arbitrarily old — a single failed beat would leave `could_not_run` standing as a report that the cadence is not firing long after it recovered.
- **claim:** §17.3: the Strategic layer refreshes "quarterly + market signal" and the Action Plan "weekly + KPI-triggered" as continuously-maintained layers — so whether the cadence is firing NOW is the fact this surface exists to answer.
- **observed:** GET /api/v1/organism/cadence returned last_beat.at = 2026-10-05T14:45:21Z with refreshed = the two boot refreshes, while /api/v1/heartbeat/status at the same moment showed 10 beats and last_beat 2026-10-05T14:53:22Z. The heartbeat assigns `self.last_cadence` only inside `if _refreshed or _cad_failed:`, so every beat on which nothing was due leaves the previous beat's record in place and the route republishes it as the latest. The cadence itself is real and heartbeat-driven, and its per-refresh provenance ("DERIVED from the plan's own stored state by this module - no model composed it") is exemplary — this is a reporting defect on top of working machinery.
- **evidence:** C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/organism/heartbeat.py:287-294 (`if _refreshed or _cad_failed:` guarding the assignment); C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/api/organism_status.py:699-723 (builds `last_beat` from `heartbeat.last_cadence` and the basis sentence). API_ONLY — no frontend file references /organism/cadence, so the reached form is this response body.
- **disclosed to the user:** No — the basis sentence states the opposite of the field's actual meaning.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Reproduced with a wider gap than the assessor measured (26 beats rather than 8). Tier 1 rather than 2 because the defect is not a missing field but a sentence the handler itself publishes: last_beat_basis states that `last_beat` is what the most recent heartbeat managed, and the named timestamp is 26 minutes older than the most recent heartbeat. `ran: true` alongside a stale `at` is an assertion about which beat this was, not a blank. Tier 1 rather than 3 because the handler goes further and instructs the reader how to draw a conclusion from the field — 'A layer that is DUE while last_beat.could_not_run is set means the cadence is not firing' — which, on a field that can be arbitrarily old, converts one historical failed beat into a standing report that the cadence is broken long after it recovered. Nothing on the surface qualifies the age.
- **refuter's evidence:** Executed GET /api/v1/organism/cadence: last_beat {ran: true, at '2026-10-05T14:45:21Z', refreshed: [strategic, action_plan], could_not_run: null, basis '2 layer(s) refreshed on this beat and 0 could not run…'} under last_beat_basis '`layers` is the plan's own record of when each layer last refreshed; `last_beat` is what the most recent heartbeat managed. A layer that is DUE while last_beat.could_not_run is set means the cadence is not firing…'. GET /api/v1/heartbeat/status read at the same moment: beats 28, last_beat '2026-10-05T15:11:24Z' — 26 minutes and roughly 26 beats later. Code: agentic_core/organism/heartbeat.py:288 guards the assignment with `if _refreshed or _cad_failed:`, so a beat on which nothing was due leaves the previous record standing; agentic_core/api/organism_status.py:699-723 builds last_beat from heartbeat.last_cadence and publishes the basis sentence. grep over apps/workstation-superapp/src for 'organism/cadence' returns nothing, so API_ONLY is correct and the response body is the reached form.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Set `last_cadence` on EVERY beat that completed the cadence step (including `{"refreshed": [], "could_not_run": None}` for "nothing was due"), or carry the beat counter and timestamp separately so the route can say "the last beat that acted on the cadence was X; the most recent beat was Y".

The same backend proves the disclosure was available and is simply absent here — business_plan.py's generate path says in words that the floor's draft is prompt echo and writes nothing, while this path renders the echo under a heading claiming it is a directive.

Render: apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/enterprise/BoardOfDirectors.tsx:339-363 ('Chief's Board Directive' and 'AI CEO Action Plan (delegated)' over the raw strings). ### R3.6 · The §17.3 Strategic and Action-Plan cadence runs only for the workstation apex — no VSB's layers are ever refreshed — **PARTIAL** · tier 1 *(assessed tier 3)*

- **severity (assessor):** medium
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** A founder who opens their entity's board pack is told, per layer, exactly this: "no §17.3 strategic refresh exists for this entity's plan, so this layer is whatever the CEO specification is - which is what the basis says" and "no §17.3 action-plan refresh exists for this entity's plan, so this layer is whatever the board roster is", and the strategic layer is marked `holds: "placeholder"`, `present: false`. The shortfall is large — it is every living enterprise the platform generates — but the surface states it in its own words at the point of reading, which the tier rules put at 3 however large it is. It is not tier 2 because nothing is left unsaid, and not tier 1 because no field asserts a refresh that did not happen (`refresh_id` is null, not a fabricated id).
- **claim:** §17.3: "Strategic (AI CEO; quarterly + market signal)" and "Action Plan (BTO; weekly + KPI-triggered)" are two of the four "continuously-maintained layers (with cadence)" of the Living Business System — and the VSB IDBO enterprise is what the vision says the layers belong to.

GET /api/v1/organism/cadence?scope=vsb-9a371566c3 confirms it by execution: both layers return `refresh_count: 0`, `ever_refreshed: false`, `last_refresh_at: null` and `due: true` with trigger "never_refreshed" — permanently due, with nothing that will ever fire them. For the apex the cadence is genuinely delivered: two refreshes on record, honest provenance, and a `due` computation that distinguishes "never" from "just now".
- **evidence:** C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/organism/heartbeat.py:275-285 (`_res = _cad.refresh("workstation", _layer)`); C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/api/vsb.py:1780-1799 (the board pack's per-layer `refresh_basis`); executed GET /api/v1/organism/cadence?scope=vsb-9a371566c3 and POST /api/v1/vsb/vsb-9a371566c3/board-pack.
- **disclosed to the user:** Yes — stated per layer in the board pack and in the cadence response itself.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). The capability gap is reproduced, but I am ESCALATING the tier from 3 to 1 because the assessor's ground for tier 3 is falsified by execution. Their stated reason was that 'no field asserts a refresh that did not happen (refresh_id is null, not a fabricated id)'. It does: within one scoped document for vsb-70c8186f, `layers` says both §17.3 layers have NEVER been refreshed and `history_count` is 0, while `last_beat` says 'ran: true' and names those same two layers — strategic and action_plan — as '2 layer(s) refreshed on this beat', with a basis sentence calling it what the most recent heartbeat managed and no word anywhere that this field belongs to a different scope. A founder reading the surface built to answer 'is my entity's cadence firing?' is told it fired minutes ago on both layers. That is an assertion, not an omission, so not tier 2; and it is not a disclosed shortfall, because the honest statements the assessor relied on live on a different surface (the board pack) while this one contradicts them. The per-layer board-pack disclosure is genuinely exemplary and I verified it — but a disclosure elsewhere does not de-tier a false assertion here.
- **refuter's evidence:** The hard-coded scope is real: agentic_core/organism/heartbeat.py:278 `_res = _cad.refresh("workstation", _layer)`, the only call site. Executed GET /api/v1/organism/cadence?scope=vsb-70c8186f: strategic {last_refresh_at null, refresh_count 0, ever_refreshed false, due true, trigger 'never_refreshed'}, action_plan identical, history_count 0. BUT the same scoped response carries last_beat {"ran": true, "at": "2026-10-05T14:45:21Z", "refreshed": [{"layer": "strategic", …, "served_by": "deterministic-floor"}, {"layer": "action_plan", …}], "could_not_run": null, "basis": "2 layer(s) refreshed on this beat and 0 could not run…"} under last_beat_basis '`last_beat` is what the most recent heartbeat managed' — no scope qualifier anywhere. Source: agentic_core/api/organism_status.py:699-712 reads the process-global heartbeat.last_cadence into a response whose scope, layers and history are all scoped. The board pack's honest counterpart is real and I read it back from POST /api/v1/vsb/vsb-70c8186f/board-pack: strategic holds 'empty', present false, refresh_basis 'no §17.3 strategic refresh exists for this entity's plan…'; action_plan holds 'other' with the roster basis.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Have the beat iterate the living registry (one entity per beat, round-robin, as the §4 economy step already does) so each VSB's two layers refresh on their own clocks; until then, the entity's cockpit should carry the board pack's sentence so a founder does not have to assemble a pack to learn it.

### R3.7 · §17.4 Mode 2 — the expert → digital-twin human node — is not built, and the Chief is honestly reported as a role rather than a twin — **PARTIAL** *(assessed MISSING)* · tier 3

- **severity (assessor):** low
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The founder's Board page renders, in amber, "A role, not a modelled twin" followed by the route's own basis: "A ROLE, NOT A MODELLED TWIN: this Owner has written no instruction and made no recorded decision, so there is nothing of theirs to model. The standing canon is carried and is the platform's, identical for every Chief, so it cannot stand in for a lived record…". The capability is absent and the surface says so in the strongest terms, including the rule by which it decides — that is tier 3, not tier 2. It is not tier 1 because no surface in this region (with the one exception reported separately at vsb.py:2351) claims a twin exists.
- **claim:** §17.4 Mode 2: "Expert → Digital-Twin human node (24/7 expertise without the expert present). This is the basis of the Chief = the founder's digital twin"; §15 principle 3 makes the twin Chief invariant.
- **observed:** GET /api/v1/board/chief/model: `is_modelled_twin: false`, `reported_as: "role"`, `owner_inputs: 0`, `instructions.count: 0`, `decisions.count: 0`, `profile.declared_by_owner: false`, plus a `method` field stating that the standing canon is never counted as the Owner's own declaration. GET /api/v1/twin/models: `{"models": [], "total": 0}` — no expert twin node of any kind exists. The route distinguishes "not loaded" from "no inputs" and the page renders the twin and role cases through two different branches, so the honesty survives a future twin as well as today's absence.
- **evidence:** Executed GET /api/v1/board/chief/model and GET /api/v1/twin/models; rendered at C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/enterprise/BoardOfDirectors.tsx:100-125 (`chiefStanding()`, data-testid chief-model-role) and carried with every directive at :348 (data-testid chief-standing-with-output).
- **disclosed to the user:** Yes — on the Board tab, beside every directive the Chief issues, and in the route's own basis and method strings.
- **refutation: REFUTED — corrected to PARTIAL.** Refuted on the evidence: the finding's whole observation — is_modelled_twin false, reported_as 'role', owner_inputs 0, and the amber 'A role, not a modelled twin' rendering — is not what any reader sees now, because one instruction and one ratification recorded during the assessment itself crossed a threshold of one. So the verdict MISSING, resting on that reading, cannot stand as written; the Chief half of Mode 2 is PARTIAL — an assembled record, not a trained model, reported under the word 'modelled'. The capability the vision names (an Expert → Digital-Twin human node, any expert, 24/7 without the expert present) is genuinely absent: zero twin models exist and nothing creates one. I hold the tier at 3 rather than raising it, and the reason is the tier rule itself: the shortfall is stated on the reached surface in the platform's own words. The Board page header reads 'no digital-twin model is trained yet (Mode 2 is planned, P3.4)', the roster card beside it repeats '(the Owner's charter and instructions — no twin model is trained)', the chip's basis names its entire input, and the Digital Twins page says nothing there is trained. Not tier 2, because nothing is left unsaid; not tier 1, because no statement is false — though 'Modelled twin' in emerald sitting on the same page as 'no digital-twin model is trained yet' is a vocabulary collision that deserves its own register row, since 'modelled' is a word nothing in the system has earned.
- **refuter's evidence:** The observed values are falsified. Executed GET /api/v1/board/chief/model: is_modelled_twin TRUE, reported_as 'modelled twin', owner_inputs 2, instructions.count 1, decisions.count 1, basis 'MODELLED TWIN: built from 1 instruction(s) the Owner wrote and 1 decision(s) the Owner made…'. The gate is agentic_core/api/board.py:208 `is_twin = (n_i + n_d) > 0`, so a single typed instruction flips it — and it also unlocks behaviour: board.py:403 refuses the unprompted twin-directive path only `if not m["is_modelled_twin"]`, and board.py:489-492 founder_profile() swaps the Chief's prompt header to 'FOUNDER MODEL (the Owner's lived record — reason AS this person)'. The page follows: apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/enterprise/BoardOfDirectors.tsx:106-113 now renders the emerald data-testid 'chief-model-twin' branch, 'Modelled twin — 1 instruction(s) you wrote · 1 decision(s) you made', beside every directive. What IS still absent: GET /api/v1/twin/models → {"models":[],"total":0}, and apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/developers/DigitalTwins.tsx:57-59 states 'Nothing here is a trained model' with 'No twins yet.' when the list is empty.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** None for honesty. The build is P3.4 and its scope is an open Owner ruling (§17.4's own note); the one thing worth doing now is extending the "no twin model is trained" phrase guard to the establish stream, which is the single surface still contradicting this route.

### R3.8 · DELIVERED — arms-length Change Control with Board ratification genuinely holds a HIGH change back, verified end to end — **DELIVERED**

- **severity (assessor):** none
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** Verdict DELIVERED, so tier 0. Every claim the chain makes was driven and the refusal was observed, not inferred from source: the 409 body named the gate, its source and the way out, and the decision basis distinguished the measured part of the composite health from the blended figure instead of reporting 1.0 as a measurement.
- **claim:** §5: "Change Control (arms-length Agency) — governs all change at arm's length from operations"; the W464 ruling that "a HIGH change approved by a review waits for Board ratification: nothing implements or applies it until the Board, on the Owner's direction, ratifies or refuses it"; §17.5's arms-length and twin-pre-validation invariants.
- **observed:** POST /api/v1/cca/submit (change_type code_change) → impact_tier HIGH. POST /api/v1/cca/{id}/review → `decision: approved`, `decision_source: health_threshold_rule`, `awaiting_board_ratification: true`, `ueg_logged: true`, and a review_result that states "DECIDED BY RULE, NOT BY THE SERVING RESOURCE" and that the floor's prose "had NO bearing on the decision". POST /api/v1/cca/{id}/implement → HTTP 409 naming the ratification requirement. GET /api/v1/board/ratifications → the row with `ratifying_board: {scope: workstation, tier: apex}` and routing text explaining that a vsb_id routes to that VSB's own board. POST /api/v1/board/ratifications/{id} {decision: ratify} → ratified, `by_verified: false` (single-user mode, honestly marked), ueg_logged. The code also keeps the honesty rule that a change carrying no config payload is marked `implementation_effect: "recorded_only"` with the basis "no platform behaviour changed", and that a set-but-unconsumed lever is NOT marked implemented.
- **evidence:** C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/api/change_control.py:1811-1820 (the ratification 409, which `?force` cannot pass) and :1917-1932 (implementation_effect / recorded_only); C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/api/board.py (ratifications queue + decision); executed against http://127.0.0.1:8031 as cca-8928c69590.
- **disclosed to the user:** Yes — the 409 states the rule, the ratification note states that a HIGH change still needs a recorded pre-validation PASS, and `by_verified: false` marks the unauthenticated principal.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Confirmed DELIVERED and tier 0 on my own execution rather than on the assessor's, and I found the chain to be STRONGER than reported on two points they did not record: ?force=true is also refused 409, so the documented admin override cannot pass the ratification gate; and the ratify POST itself refuses until the caller states on_owner_direction, which is the W464 ruling's 'the caller states it is the Owner's direction' clause actually enforced rather than described. Every claim in the chain was driven, every refusal observed rather than inferred from source, and the two places where the system could have flattered itself — the composite health figure and the effect of implementing a prose-only change — both report the honest narrower fact instead (the measured 40% rather than the blended 1.0; 'recorded_only … no platform behaviour changed'). Tier 0 because the verdict is DELIVERED, and the tier rules reserve 0 for exactly that.
- **refuter's evidence:** Driven independently end to end as cca-8bcb4165b1. POST /api/v1/cca/submit {change_type: code_change} → impact_tier HIGH, status submitted. POST /api/v1/cca/{id}/review {reviewer_notes} → decision approved, decision_source 'health_threshold_rule', status approved, awaiting_board_ratification true, ueg_logged true, review_result 'DECIDED BY RULE, NOT BY THE SERVING RESOURCE: no [DECISION: …] marker was returned … MEASURED composite_health 1.00 >= 0.5 → approved (that is the measured part, 40% of the composite's weight; the blended figure 1.0 carries 2 unmeasured term(s) (self healing health, metabolic)…)' and '…The prose below was written by Workstation's own deterministic native engine (the floor, which is not a model) and had NO bearing on the decision.' POST …/implement → HTTP 409 'This HIGH change was approved by a review (the organism-health threshold rule), so it waits for the Board to ratify it on the Owner's direction before it is implemented…'. POST …/implement?force=true → ALSO 409. GET /api/v1/board/ratifications → the row with ratifying_board {scope workstation, tier apex, represents_owner Rehan} and twin_prevalidation {verdict pass, source 'health_gate_default', source_label 'no twin model — health gate only'}. POST /api/v1/board/ratifications/{id} {decision: ratify} → 400 'A ratification is the Owner's decision, recorded by the Board: resend with on_owner_direction: true.' With that flag → ratified, by 'single-user-mode', by_verified false, ueg_logged true. POST …/implement then succeeded; the record reads status implemented, implementation_effect 'recorded_only', implementation_effect_basis 'this change carried nothing for the platform to apply … no platform behaviour changed.' Code: agentic_core/api/change_control.py:1811-1820, :1917-1932.
- **residual lead (assessor's note on a DELIVERED entry — a lead, not a defect):** None.

### R3.9 · DELIVERED — Mode 3 human review gates genuinely refuse every lifecycle mover, and the Board Pack is assembled fresh with per-layer honesty — **PARTIAL** *(assessed DELIVERED)* · tier 2 *(assessed tier 0)*

- **severity (assessor):** none
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** Verdict DELIVERED, so tier 0. The gate was driven rather than read: two different movers were refused with the gate pending, and the pack's layer states were checked against the entity's actual stored fields.
- **claim:** §17.4 Mode 3: "optional human review gates at any Concept→Commercialisation stage (set in the VSB genome)"; §17.3: "Board Pack (on-demand): assembled fresh from live data, DCS-registered."
- **observed:** POST /api/v1/vsb/vsb-9a371566c3/review-gates {stages:[build,commercialise]} then POST …/board-pack → HTTP 409 `{"error": "review gate blocks progress — a human review is pending (Mode 3); board pack refused", "gate": "build", "blocking": [build, commercialise], "clear_by": "POST …/review-gates/build/decision {\"decision\": \"approve\"} — or ungate the stage"}`, and POST /api/v1/swarm/cascade scoped to the same entity → the identical 409 for mover "cascade". The shared guard `_refuse_gated` has 13 call sites covering ship, repo, website, web app, phone app, board pack, evolve, evolution apply, repo cascade, objective orchestrate, contract delivery and swarm run. With the gates cleared, the pack returned `layers_present: [constitutional, operational]` and marked the other two honestly: strategic `holds: "placeholder"` with the basis "the CEO specification on this entity is the platform's pending-body text, not a specification anyone composed", action_plan `holds: "other"` with "the entity's standing board roster, which is who would act and not what is to be done". Values read "NOT DECLARED - this entity has declared no values of its own" with a source field explaining that the platform's standing values line is deliberately NOT shown in their place; `entity_compliance` reads `never_screened: true`; `delivery_coverage: 0.0` with `not_assessable: true`; `dcs_registered: true` with a SHA-512 hash and a version number.
- **evidence:** C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/api/vsb.py:1809-1821 (`_refuse_gated`), :1591-1606 (`blocks_progress` / `_gates_blocking`), :1322 and :2401 (two of the 13 call sites); executed POSTs to /api/v1/vsb/vsb-9a371566c3/review-gates, …/board-pack and /api/v1/swarm/cascade. §17.4's own caveat still holds and is accurate: because the genesis journey runs every stage in one request before the entity exists, a gate pauses the ship rather than a stage mid-journey.
- **disclosed to the user:** Yes — the refusal names the gate, its status and the way out; every pack layer carries its own `holds` and `basis`.
- **refutation: REFUTED — corrected to PARTIAL.** Refuted as a DELIVERED finding: 'genuinely refuse every lifecycle mover, verified end to end' does not hold, because on a Studio-spawned entity Mode 3 cannot be configured at all and the mover I tried ran to completion with HTTP 200. The assessor verified one entity and generalised; checking a second entity from a different birth path broke it, and the thirteen _refuse_gated call sites they counted are all downstream of a gate that can never be written there. The Board Pack half of the finding I confirm — layers_present ['constitutional','operational'], strategic holds 'empty', action_plan holds 'other' with the roster basis, values 'NOT DECLARED - this entity has declared no values of its own', entity_compliance never_screened true, delivery_coverage 0.0 with not_assessable true, dcs_registered with a SHA-512 hash — that part is genuinely delivered and honest per layer. Tier 2 rather than the assessor's 0: the shortfall is real and the surface does not state it. A founder is told 'failed with KeyError … the cause is not established', which discloses that the platform does not know why, not that Mode 3 is unavailable for this entity — and the GET beside it keeps answering 200 with an empty stage list, which reads as 'no gates set' rather than 'no gates possible'. Not tier 1, because no field asserts a gate that is not there: the failure is reported as a failure, and the board pack that followed made no claim about having been screened by a gate.
- **refuter's evidence:** The gate works for one class of entity and CANNOT BE SET AT ALL for another. Working half, driven: POST /api/v1/vsb/vsb-9a371566c3/review-gates {stages:[build]} → 200, stages ['build']; POST …/board-pack → HTTP 409 {error 'review gate blocks progress — a human review is pending (Mode 3); board pack refused', mover 'board pack', gate 'build', status 'pending', blocks_progress true, clear_by 'POST …/review-gates/build/decision {"decision": "approve"} — or ungate the stage'} (I then cleared the gate). Broken half, driven twice: POST /api/v1/vsb/vsb-70c8186f/review-gates {stages:[build,commercialise]} and again {stages:[build]} → {"detail":"POST /api/v1/vsb/vsb-70c8186f/review-gates failed with KeyError. The failure is recorded; the cause is not established.","failure_class":"KeyError","cause_established":false}, while GET on the same path returns 200 with stages [] — and POST /api/v1/vsb/vsb-70c8186f/board-pack then returned HTTP 200, the mover running unguarded. Root cause located: agentic_core/api/vsb.py:85-86 `def _save_vsb(vsb): atomic_write_json(_vsb_path(vsb["vsb_id"]), vsb)` indexes a key that entity does not have — GET /api/v1/vsb/vsb-70c8186f returns keys ['board','business_plan_scope','challenge','created_at','domain','duration_seconds','economy','entity_id','living','owner_id','realm','solution_name','stages','status','synthesis_id'], entity_id 'vsb-70c8186f', NO vsb_id. agentic_core/api/synthesis_studio.py:45 binds the SAME store (`_VSB_STORE = data_path("vsb_entities")`) and :66 saves with `entity["entity_id"]`, :308-310 and :403 writing records keyed entity_id only. So every Studio-spawned entity lands in the store that api/vsb.py manages, and every api/vsb.py surface that calls _save_vsb raises on it.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** None.

## R4 — §6 + §7 + §17.2 — the native AI mandate, the reconfigurable resource fabric, the seven biomimetic layers

**Assessor's region summary:** R4 (§6 native AI mandate, §7 reconfigurable resource fabric, §17.2 the seven biomimetic layers), measured against a live backend at 127.0.0.1:8031 by executing ~40 routes and reading the handlers and the components that render them. I could not drive a browser, so every UI judgement below is reasoned from apps/workstation-superapp/src and says which file and line a user's text comes from.  Is the AI genuinely native and in-house-first? Orchestration and the swarm: yes, and verifiably. The tree, the linear cascade, the twelve primitives and all 13 self-checked modules run in this process with no network call; external providers are double-gated (a key AND AI_ALLOW_EXTERNAL) and reported as `is_external: true` accelerants, never as a fallback the platform depends on. Models: no. The "owned" estate is a deterministic string-template composer plus optional third-party open weights on Ollama; nothing in the repo authors, trains or ships a Workstation model, and `/native-ai/lifecycle` holds an empty estate. The system says this honestly at every surface; §6's prose does not (finding 6).  Are cascades bespoke and user-reconfigurable? Two of §6's three named places deliver it (the Resource Fabric's per-resource params, the Forge's per-stage config, and the real define/edit/run/retire cascade designer on the Primitive Console — finding 9); Build-to-Order has none of it and says nothing (finding 4). Bespoke-per-founder is partial via `_living_vsb_grounding`, which prepends a VSB-bound cascade's current stage, mission and Chief — not a trained twin.  Which of the seven layers are real? Genome, Nervous, Immune, self-healing and Musculoskeletal have running implementations I executed; Cardiovascular is a host-CPU reading under an anatomical name (finding 3); Respiratory's real surface (the Agent Hub) is an honest empty bus and the module the canon maps beside it cannot be imported; Endocrine works as the biobus while the regulator the map names is imported by nothing (finding 5).  Is provenance honest everywhere output reaches a user? Nearly — and the gap is exactly where the question pointed. The Synthesis-Studio cascade's nine SSE stage events, its `done` frame and the nine-stage VSB record it writes carry no served_by/is_external at all, two lines under a handler comment asserting that they do (finding 2). The biggest single defect is not provenance but content: a user-designed cascade's stages after the first print the engine's own boilerplate under the heading "Terms most frequent in your request" (finding 1) — the only tier 1 I could substantiate.  I HIT THE TEN-FINDING CAP, so ten is not the size of the gap. Named but not given a slot, each verified: `/api/v1/cognitive/engines` returns a self-contradictory basis sentence ("the 0 meta engines are PLANNED (no module yet)") beside its own `layers_implemented: {meta: 3}`; the cognitive engines all return fixed markers (`alignment: 0.99`, `threat_score: 0.01`, `computes: false`) and the cascade runs 6 of the canon's 9 — disclosed in the same body and reached by no UI, so API_ONLY/tier 3; `EngineType` declares 23 engines of which 11 are `declared_but_absent`; the "Autonomous workflow tree" card promises a swarm that "autonomously decomposes" the goal before the run, corrected only afterwards by the "planned by template" badge; `SynthesisStudio.tsx:459` still defaults `r.served_by ?? 'native'` for its aggregate badge — the exact guess the badge helper's own W490 comment says was removed (latent only because /synthesis/stream does send served_by); and the floor truncates one heading to "## Terms most frequent in your reques" in composed output. A reader must not take ten as all there was.

### R4.0 · A user-designed swarm cascade tells the user that the engine's own boilerplate is what their request was about — **PARTIAL** · tier 1

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The reader is shown, as stage 2 and stage 3 of a cascade they designed: "## Terms most frequent in your request … The list below is the request's most frequent terms, not findings about them. - external dependency - structured engine - owned external - engine owned - workstation" — for a request whose context I typed as "Launch a halal food delivery business in Birmingham serving 50 restaurants". Stage 1 got it right (halal food · business birmingham · serving restaurants). That is not an omission and not an environment fact: it is a positive sentence about the user's own input that is false, on the surface that renders it verbatim. It is above tier 2 because the shortfall is not merely undisclosed — the surface makes an affirmative, wrong claim about the reader's words. It is not below tier 1 on the argument that the terms are true of the stage's internal prompt, because nothing a reader can see distinguishes 'the request' from the engine's prior-context block, and the user's subject has silently vanished from the cascade by stage 2.
- **claim:** §6: the AI Agent Swarm cascade is "synthesised, modelled and simulated bespoke to each solution's needs" and is "Reconfigurable by users … in Synthesis Lab, Build-to-Order, and Forge"; §15.6 honesty over polish, never fabricate.
- **observed:** `orchestrator.swarm` sets `carry = res["output"]` and prepends it to the next stage as "Prior context:\n" + carry[:1200]. The deterministic floor counts n-grams over the WHOLE prompt, so from stage 2 onward the most frequent terms are the floor's own banner ("_[Workstation native structured engine — owned, no external dependency]_") rather than the user's subject. Verified by execution on POST /api/v1/resources/swarm/run with three designed stages and a Birmingham halal-delivery context; also on POST /api/v1/native-ai/swarm. The workflow TREE does not have this defect — `_plan_tree` restates "Overall goal: …" in every node's task, so every tree node's terms were the goal's terms.
- **evidence:** C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/ai/native/orchestrator.py:900-906 (prompt = … 'Prior context:\n' + carry[:1200] …; carry = res["output"] at :908); C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/ai/native/engine.py:205 and :302-304 (the two headings that print the term list). Reached at C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/developers/NativeAI.tsx:72 (`Trace` renders `s.output` whole, for Quick swarm and for every saved-cascade Run) and in the Resource-Fabric composition trace.
- **disclosed to the user:** No. Nothing on the stage card, and nothing in the response, says that stages after the first count the engine's own text instead of the request.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Tier 1 upheld. The reader is shown an affirmative sentence about their own words — 'the request's most frequent terms' / 'Structured frame over the terms most frequent in the request' — immediately under a 'Subject:' line quoting the instruction they typed, and the list is the engine's own banner ('external dependency', 'structured engine', 'owned external', 'workstation'). That is not an omission and not the AI_DISABLE_LOCAL environment: the floor is entitled to say it counted terms, it is not entitled to say whose terms. It is above tier 2 because the surface makes a positive false claim rather than failing to disclose one; it is not below tier 1 on the defence that the terms are true of the internal prompt, because nothing a reader can see separates 'the request' from the prior-context block, and their subject has silently left the cascade by stage 2. I verified the reach myself rather than taking it on the code citation, and I found the same class in the tree's final output, which the finding wrongly excluded.
- **refuter's evidence:** Reproduced by execution. POST /api/v1/native-ai/swarm with three stages (researcher/strategist/planner) on a Birmingham halal-delivery subject: stage 1 terms were the subject's (serving restaurants · birmingham serving · market birmingham · delivery market · food delivery); stage 2 and stage 3 printed 'Subject: Design the go-to-market strategy for that halal delivery business' and then 'Structured frame over the terms most frequent in the request (a term count, not an analysis): - external dependency - structured engine - owned external - engine owned - workstation'. Code confirmed: C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/ai/native/orchestrator.py:900-908 (carry = res['output']; prompt prepends 'Prior context:\n' + carry[:1200]) and C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/ai/native/engine.py:205, :302-304 (the two headings that print the term list). Reach verified in source: C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/developers/NativeAI.tsx:70 renders s.output per stage (line-clamp-4) and :201 renders run.final (line-clamp-6) — I counted the rendered lines of a real run and the false sentence plus its first boilerplate bullet fall inside the six-line clamp, so a user does see it. ONE CLAIM OF THIS FINDING IS WRONG AND THE DEFECT IS WIDER THAN REPORTED: the assessor exempted the workflow tree, but POST /api/v1/native-ai/tree on 'Establish a solar microgrid cooperative' returned final = 'Subject: ## Terms most frequent in your request (domain: the stated domain). (domain: the stated domain).' — the tree's synthesis takes the engine's own HEADING as the user's subject.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** In `orchestrator.swarm`, pass the ORIGINAL context (and the stage instruction) as the term-bearing subject and carry the prior output under a separately-labelled block the floor excludes from its n-gram count — the tree already does this by restating the goal in every node; or, failing that, strip the floor banner from `carry` before it becomes the next prompt and label the list "terms most frequent in this stage's input".

### R4.1 · The Synthesis-Studio cascade emits nine stage events and writes a nine-stage VSB record with no provenance field anywhere, while its own handler comment says every event carries provenance — **API_ONLY** *(assessed PARTIAL)* · tier 3 *(assessed tier 2)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** What a reader of this surface is given, per stage, is exactly `{stage, stage_key, stage_label, stage_num, content}` — and the final frame is `{stage:"done", stages_completed:9, duration_ms, entity_id, solution_name, synthesis_id}`. "stages_completed: 9" is an outcome claim with no attribution beside it, and the stored record's `stages` object is nine bare strings (0 occurrences of served_by or is_external in the whole entity). The provenance channel the task asks about does not exist on this path — that is assessable independently of AI_DISABLE_LOCAL, so it is not the environment. It stays off tier 1 only because the floor happens to stamp its own banner inside each content string, so today's reader does learn the floor composed it; it is above tier 3 because nothing on the surface states the gap, and the handler's own comment asserts the opposite.
- **claim:** §6: external providers are "optional accelerants, never dependencies" — which is only checkable if every output says what served it; §15.6 "honesty over polish (never fabricate; label simulation)".
- **observed:** POST /api/v1/studio/synthesise produced 20 SSE frames (init, 9×start, 9×complete, done). I parsed every frame: `served_by` and `is_external` are ABSENT from all 20. The handler computes `_stage_served = _st.get("served_by")` and `_stage_ext = bool(_st.get("is_external"))` and then never references either variable — they are dead assignments, two lines under a comment claiming the opposite. The durable record GET /api/v1/studio/vsb/vsb-0a6ca2ab stores `stages` as nine plain strings; `json.dumps(entity).count('served_by') == 0`. The repo has a canonical helper for exactly this (`agentic_core/api/_ai_provenance.py`, returning `{posture, served_by, is_external}`) which twelve other routers use and this one does not. No frontend file references `studio/synthesise` or `studio/vsb`, so this is API_ONLY and its reached form is the response stream plus the stored record.
- **evidence:** C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/api/synthesis_studio.py:295-305 — the comment "W506 (P2.2) - an SSE engine carries provenance on EVERY stage event, which the item names explicitly; a reader watching the stream must not have to wait for the final record." sits directly above `_stage_served`/`_stage_ext` (:302-303) and the `yield` at :305 whose payload omits both. The record written at :312-333 stores `"stages": results` where `results[stage_key] = stage_content` (:301).
- **refutation: REFUTED — corrected to API_ONLY.** Refuted as tiered and the verdict corrected from PARTIAL to API_ONLY; tier LOWERED 2 to 3. Tier 2 requires that the surface not say so, and on the reached form named by the task — the response body and the stored record — every one of the nine stages states in its own text that Workstation's native structured engine composed it with no external dependency. The absent served_by field is a machine-readable omission, and the task's own rule is that an omission is not an assertion; a code comment is not a surface, so the handler's contradicted comment cannot carry a tier. What survives is the real gap: a nine-stage cascade that spawns a durable VSB entity (board, economy, business_plan_scope, project record — I read the stored entity) and that no UI reaches, which is tier 3 'a surface nobody reaches'. It is not tier 2 because nothing is said untruly and nothing material is hidden from the only reader this path has; the latent risk — that a non-floor resource would leave no banner and therefore no disclosure at all — is unreachable in this environment and is the environment's doing, not the handler's.
- **refuter's evidence:** The finding's raw facts are true and I reproduced them: POST /api/v1/studio/synthesise returned 20 SSE frames (init, 9x start, 9x complete, done); I parsed every frame and served_by / is_external appear in NONE of them; the stored record GET /api/v1/studio/vsb/vsb-70c8186f has stages as nine plain strings with json.dumps(entity).count('served_by') == 0; and C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/api/synthesis_studio.py:302-303 assigns _stage_served/_stage_ext and grep shows those names occur nowhere else in the file — dead assignments under a comment at :295-296 claiming the opposite. BUT THE DISCLOSURE PREMISE FAILS: I checked each of the nine 'complete' frames for the floor's banner and all nine content strings open with '_[Workstation native structured engine — owned, no external dependency]_', and all nine strings in the durable record do too. Provenance travels WITH the output on this path, in prose, in both the stream and the stored entity. AND THE VERDICT IS WRONG: grep over apps/workstation-superapp/src for 'api/v1/studio', 'studio/synthesise' and 'studio/vsb' returns nothing, and the page named Synthesis Studio (src/pages/synthesis/SynthesisStudio.tsx) calls /api/v1/ingest/*, /api/v1/synthesis/history, /api/v1/synthesis/stream and /api/v1/synthesis/generate — a different router. Nothing in the UI reaches this nine-stage cascade.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Add `"served_by": _stage_served, "is_external": _stage_ext` to the `complete` frame at synthesis_studio.py:305, and store `stages` as `{key: {content, served_by, is_external}}` (or add a parallel `stage_provenance` map) so the record carries what served each stage; the two values are already computed.

### R4.2 · The global shell's "Cardiovascular" dot is host CPU headroom, turns green when the host is idle, and says so only in a source comment — **PARTIAL** · tier 2

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** What the reader is shown on every page is a pulsing dot whose only label — its tooltip — is the single word "Cardiovascular", green whenever the figure is above 80. The figure is `100 − host cpu%`, so the dot is greenest exactly when nothing is happening. §17.2 defines Cardiovascular as "dynamic resource allocation (distribute)", so a green dot under that name reads as the organism's own distribution being healthy. It is tier 2 and not tier 3 because the honest basis exists — the API returns `resource_flow_basis: "host CPU headroom (100 − cpu%) — spare capacity, not workload"`, the hook carries it, and the Introspection page relabelled the same figure "Host CPU headroom" — but none of it reaches this dot, so the shortfall is invisible precisely where it is most reached. It is not tier 1 because a coloured dot plus one anatomical word is a mislabel rather than an asserted figure or a certification.
- **claim:** §17.2 layer 4: "Cardiovascular — dynamic resource allocation (distribute)"; §15.6 honesty over polish.
- **observed:** `cardioClass(flow)` returns emerald above 80, teal 40-80, red below 40, from `biometrics.cardiovascular.resource_flow`, which `app_mvp.py` computes as `100 − cpu_percent` and returns alongside `resource_flow_basis` and `host_cpu_percent`. The React hook reads both basis fields (useWorkstationBiometrics.ts:92-93) and the shell dot uses neither. `agentic_core/vbs/quality.py:55-58` records the layer's own state as "measurement_under_this_name … That is host CPU headroom, honestly measured, under an anatomical name — there is no circulatory subsystem" — so the platform knows this and the dot does not say it. The same figure WAS relabelled on one surface (Introspection.tsx:121 renders `label="Host CPU headroom"`), which makes the shell dot a second, unfixed writer of the same claim.
- **evidence:** C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/BiometricStatus.tsx:167 and :192 — `title="Cardiovascular"`, the only user-visible text; the honest sentence "It says nothing about whether the platform is doing any work" is at :7-10, in a JSDoc comment. Rendered globally at C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/layout/Shell.tsx:296. Backend basis at C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/app_mvp.py:645-649.
- **disclosed to the user:** No — the disclosure is in a code comment and in two API fields the component ignores.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Tier 2 upheld, verdict upheld. On every page of the app a reader gets a pulsing dot whose entire label is the word 'Cardiovascular', and §17.2 defines that layer as 'dynamic resource allocation (distribute)' — so the colour reads as the organism's own distribution being healthy, when the number is 100 minus host CPU and is greenest exactly when the platform is doing nothing (the same payload says platform_busy: false while flow sat at 76.2). It is tier 2 rather than tier 3 because the honest basis exists and is one field away — the API returns it, the hook carries it, a second page already renders the same figure as 'Host CPU headroom' — and none of it reaches the one surface that is on every screen, so the shortfall is invisible precisely where it is most reached. It is not tier 1 because a coloured dot plus one anatomical word mislabels rather than asserts: no figure is shown and no outcome is certified. The §17.2 layer itself is not empty — homeostasis.assess really did cap parallelism (max_parallel 3, posture 'reduced') on the tree run I executed — which is why PARTIAL, not STUB.

### R4.3 · Build-to-Order offers no swarm-cascade reconfiguration, although §6 names it beside Synthesis Lab and Forge as a place users redesign cascades — **PARTIAL** · tier 3 *(assessed tier 2)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The Build-to-Order surface is partial against the clause that names it, and it says nothing about that. It is tier 2 rather than tier 3 because the gap is not disclosed and the page gives no pointer to where the capability does live; it is not tier 1 because nothing on the page asserts the capability — it simply isn't there, and no figure or outcome is misstated.

Synthesis Lab / Resource Fabric: ResourceFabric.tsx exposes `reconfigurable_params` per resource with save-and-re-simulate, and NativeAI.tsx holds the real cascade designer. Forge: ForgePipeline.tsx added per-stage free-text `config` and reports unreadable lines rather than dropping them. Build-to-Order: `grep -c 'cascade|swarm|reconfigur'` over the 466-line BTOCatalog.tsx returns zero; GET /api/v1/bto/components returns nine components (entity, organism, vsb, csuite, coe, domains, realms, products, services) and no cascade or swarm; POST /api/v1/bto/configure returns a blueprint with `components`, `product_resources`, `not_buildable` and no cascade field.
- **evidence:** C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/BTOCatalog.tsx (no cascade/swarm/reconfigure token in the file); C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/api/bto.py components list, verified live at GET /api/v1/bto/components. Contrast C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/developers/NativeAI.tsx:931-968 (the working designer, anchored at #cascade-designer) and ForgePipeline.tsx:31-47 (per-stage config).
- **disclosed to the user:** No.
- **refutation: REFUTED — corrected to PARTIAL.** Refuted, verdict kept at PARTIAL but tier LOWERED 2 to 3. Tier 2 rested on two assertions I disproved by reading the file the finding cites: that the page gives no pointer to where cascade reconfiguration lives, and that it says nothing about its own scope. It does both, in its header — a labelled link reading 'select · reconfigure · combine' and a subtitle limiting the page to specification. What remains true is a real gap: §6 names Build-to-Order beside Synthesis Lab and Forge, and stage-level cascade design exists only on the developers' Native-AI page (#cascade-designer), not inside BTO. That is a capability gap with a disclosed scope and a one-click pointer, which is tier 3. It is not tier 2 because the reader is neither misled about what the page does nor left without the route to the capability, and it is not tier 1 because the page asserts no figure and no outcome. It is not DELIVERED because the clause names BTO specifically and BTO itself edits no stages.
- **refuter's evidence:** The absence is real: grep -ciE 'cascade|swarm|reconfigur' over the 466-line C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/BTOCatalog.tsx returns 0, and GET /api/v1/bto/components returns exactly nine structural components (entity, organism, vsb, csuite, coe, domains, realms, products, services) with no cascade or swarm. BUT THE TWO REASONS GIVEN FOR TIER 2 ARE BOTH FALSE, and the grep is why. BTOCatalog.tsx:12 imports FabricLink and :135 renders it in the page HEADER, three lines under the h1. C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/FabricLink.tsx:11-15 is <Link to="/resource-fabric"> whose visible text is 'Resource Fabric — select · reconfigure · combine' and whose title is 'Open the reconfigurable Resource Fabric — access, select, reconfigure and combine resources, model & simulate before commit'. So 'no link to the one that exists' is wrong — the word 'reconfigure' is in FabricLink.tsx, not in BTOCatalog.tsx, which is exactly what a one-file grep cannot see. And the destination does carry the capability: src/pages/synthesis/ResourceFabric.tsx:643-646 is a 'Run on the native swarm (§6)' block and :703-730 renders the real Chief->Build-to-Order org_cascade result. The page also scopes itself on screen at :132-134: 'Sovereign Entity Configurator · Select components · Compose your blueprint (specification only — nothing is provisioned)'.
