### R4.5 · No model in the estate is Workstation's own — the owned tier is a deterministic string-template floor plus third-party open weights on a third-party runtime — **PARTIAL** *(assessed DOC_OVERCLAIM)* · tier 3

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** Every system surface I executed states this correctly, so no reader of the running platform is misled: `/native-ai/status` returns `is_real_model: false`, `mode: "deterministic_floor"`, and "The deterministic native floor is serving — honest structured reasoning, NOT an LLM"; `/native-ai/models` returns `local_models: []`; `/native-ai/tiers` says the domain-specialist tier is NOT_RUNNABLE and spells out "short by 4.31 GB". The overclaim is in the canon's prose, not on a reached surface, which is why this is tier 3 and not tier 1 — and it is not tier 2 because the system discloses the shortfall at every point of use.
- **claim:** §6: "Native, owned capability — not a façade over third-party APIs. Workstation's own models, its own orchestration, its own swarm"; and "owned/locally-runnable models".
- **observed:** Orchestration and the swarm ARE Workstation's own and in-process (verified: `/native-ai/selfcheck` → 13 of 13 owned modules live; the tree, the cascade and the twelve primitives all run in-process with no network call). Models are not. The always-available "native" resource is `agentic_core/ai/native/engine.py`, a keyword-branching string composer — it does n-gram counting and emits headings; it does not infer. The only other non-external tier is `ollama`, which is a third-party runtime serving third-party open weights (`configured_model: "llama3.2"`), reported as `kind: "local", is_external: false, note: "Self-hosted local model(s) — owned capability when running"`. Nothing in the repo trains, fine-tunes, distils or ships a Workstation-authored model; `/native-ai/lifecycle` holds `promoted_default: null, discovered: [], active_estate: []`. So "its own models" is, in the system, "self-hosted other people's models, optional, plus a template".
- **evidence:** Live: GET /api/v1/native-ai/status, /models, /tiers, /lifecycle on http://127.0.0.1:8031. Code: C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/ai/native/engine.py (the floor's branch table, e.g. :280-304); C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/ai/native/orchestrator.py:347-360 (`_run_model` dispatches only to ollama/external).
- **disclosed to the user:** Yes, consistently, at every surface that reports what served.
- **refutation: REFUTED — corrected to PARTIAL.** Refuted on the verdict; tier UNCHANGED at 3. DOC_OVERCLAIM requires the canon to claim more than the system does, and here the canon defines its own term one paragraph later as 'owned/locally-runnable models' and states the goal as independence from third-party APIs rather than authorship of weights — so reading 'its own models' as 'models Workstation trained' is stricter than §6 reads itself, and I will not charge an overclaim on a stricter reading than the document's own. What is genuinely short is the estate: no locally-runnable model is installed here, so only the deterministic floor serves, and that is AI_DISABLE_LOCAL plus an empty model directory — the environment. Hence PARTIAL. Tier 3 is right and it is not tier 2 because every surface I executed volunteers the shortfall unprompted (is_real_model false, mode deterministic_floor, local_models [], 'short by' the RAM figure on the tier that cannot run), and not tier 1 because nothing anywhere claims a model inferred when the floor composed.
- **refuter's evidence:** Every measurement reproduced. GET /api/v1/native-ai/models: local_models [], default_local null, promoted_default null. /lifecycle: promoted_default null, discovered [], active_estate [], serving 'native_floor', serving_basis 'no local model is installed, so the deterministic native floor serves; llama3.2 configured but absent'. /status: is_real_model false, mode 'deterministic_floor', 'The deterministic native floor is serving — honest structured reasoning, NOT an LLM'; the ollama entry reads kind 'local', is_external false, available false. /tiers: domain_specialist short of RAM on a 7.69 GB machine, with cpu_basis warning that os.cpu_count 'is not a measure of how fast any of them is'. /selfcheck: 13 of 13 owned modules live, all in-process. The floor at agentic_core/ai/native/engine.py is a keyword branch table that composes strings and counts n-grams. BUT THE OVERCLAIM IS CHARGED AGAINST A SENTENCE §6 ITSELF OPERATIONALISES: the same section's architectural-implication paragraph states the requirement as 'owned/locally-runnable models, an in-platform orchestrator, and a swarm engine', and frames the whole mandate as 'not a façade over third-party APIs ... so it is not dependent, not exposed to others' cost/latency/staleness'. Self-hosted open weights on a local runtime are not a façade over a third-party API and introduce no provider dependency, which is the property the mandate names; the ollama tier implements it and would serve if a model were pulled.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Change §6's "Workstation's own models" to the measured claim — "Workstation's own orchestration and swarm, with a self-hosted model estate (open weights) and a deterministic owned floor; no Workstation-trained model exists" — so the canon stops promising an artefact that no plan item builds.

### R4.6 · The ensemble route returns a constant method string claiming parallelism and consensus synthesis that did not happen, with no basis field saying so — **PARTIAL** · tier 3

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The response body says `"method": "parallel ensemble across owned models → consensus synthesis"` while reporting `models_run: ["native"]`, one member, and `synthesis: null` — one resource, no parallelism, no synthesis. Every neighbouring primitive in this router carries a `basis` that says what could not be assessed (`/decide`: "the utility function scored all 3 actions identically … so no action was selected"; `/entropy`: "no sources were supplied, so this is the fixed genesis-constant seed"); this one carries none. It lands on tier 3 rather than tier 2 because the page that reaches it states the truth plainly — NativeAI.tsx computes "Ensemble · 1 of 1 owned models produced output · no consensus synthesised (it needs two or more members that produced output)" — so the overstatement survives only in the API body. It is not tier 1 because `method` names a declared procedure rather than asserting a produced result, and the null synthesis beside it contradicts it in the same object.
- **claim:** §6: the native resource layer's cascades are "verified, tested and validated"; §15.6 honesty over polish.
- **observed:** POST /api/v1/native-ai/ensemble with `{"prompt":"Rank three options for rural energy"}` returned `models_run: ["native"]`, one member served by native, `synthesis: null`, `method: "parallel ensemble across owned models → consensus synthesis"`. In the code the synthesis step is gated on `if synthesize and len(produced) > 1`, so with one owned resource it is structurally unreachable, yet `method` is a literal returned unconditionally. The button that triggers it carries a static promise too — `title="Run across ALL owned models in parallel, then synthesise a consensus"` — corrected only by the label that appears after the run.
- **evidence:** C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/ai/native/orchestrator.py:333-345 (`synthesis = None`; `if synthesize and len(produced) > 1:`; then the unconditional `"method": "parallel ensemble across owned models → consensus synthesis"`). The honest page label at C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/developers/NativeAI.tsx:794-805; the static tooltip at :779.
- **disclosed to the user:** On the page, yes. In the API body, no.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Tier 3 upheld, verdict upheld. The response body overstates — 'parallel ensemble across owned models -> consensus synthesis' beside models_run ['native'], one member and synthesis null — and it is the one primitive in this router with no basis string, which is a genuine shortfall against the §15.6 pattern its neighbours all follow. It stays at tier 3 and does not rise to tier 2 because the surface a person actually reaches states the truth plainly and computes it from the run ('Ensemble · 1 of 1 owned models produced output · no consensus synthesised'), so the overstatement survives only in the body; and it is not tier 1 because `method` names a declared procedure rather than asserting a produced result, and synthesis: null sits in the same object contradicting it — a reader of the body holds the correction in hand, which is not the case in finding 0.
- **refuter's evidence:** Reproduced verbatim. POST /api/v1/native-ai/ensemble {'prompt':'Rank three options for rural energy'} returned models_run ['native'], one member served_by native, synthesis null, and method 'parallel ensemble across owned models -> consensus synthesis'. Code confirmed at C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/agentic_core/ai/native/orchestrator.py:333-345: synthesis = None, then `if synthesize and len(produced) > 1:` — structurally unreachable with one owned resource — then the method literal in the unconditional return dict. The page's correction is real: apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/developers/NativeAI.tsx:802-803 renders ' -> consensus synthesised' or ' · no consensus synthesised (it needs two or more members that produced output)'; the static pre-run promise at :779 (title='Run across ALL owned models in parallel, then synthesise a consensus') is also real. I confirmed the contrast the finding draws: /decide returns selected_action null with utility_source 'default survival utility — it does NOT read the action, so with 2+ actions every candidate ties and no winner is invented' and discriminated false; this route carries no basis field at all.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Compute `method` from what ran (e.g. "single owned resource — no parallelism; consensus synthesis needs two or more members that produced output") and add the `basis` field the sibling primitives all carry.

### R4.7 · Nineteen of the twenty-three organism configuration levers are stored-only switches that change nothing — **PARTIAL** · tier 3

'ok' : 'dim'}>{k.wired ? 'wired' : 'stored-only'}</Chip>`), :597 (a suggestion badged "changes nothing"), and :576 (history rows marked "· stored-only").

- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Tier 3 upheld, verdict upheld. The gap is large — §7's 'user design control' over the organism is four levers out of twenty-three — but the tiering rule is explicit that a shortfall the surface itself states is tier 3 however large, and this surface states it per key, with the reason, in the chip beside the toggle and in a sentence above the list. No dead switch is passed off as a control, which keeps it off tier 2; nothing asserts an outcome the platform did not produce, which keeps it well off tier 1. It is not tier 0 because nineteen of the twenty-three levers the page offers change nothing, including every features.* switch and all six domains.* pairs.
- **refuter's evidence:** Reproduced exactly. GET /api/v1/organism/config returns key_wiring with 23 entries of which 4 are wired: gateway.rpm_limit, organism.metabolic_throttle, organism.immune_quarantine, organism.evolution_auto_apply — the same four listed in governed_keys. Every other row carries consumer 'stored-only — nothing reads this key back', and the payload's own note reads 'only the wired keys change live behaviour; stored-only keys persist but nothing consults them — displayed so a settings panel cannot pass a dead switch off as a control'. Surface disclosure verified line by line at C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/organism/OrganismAnatomy.tsx:486-490 (the heading 'Reconfiguration — the wiring truth' and 'Only wired keys change live behaviour; stored-only keys are displayed so a dead switch can never pass as a control'), :503 (<Chip tone={k.wired ? 'ok' : 'dim'}>{k.wired ? 'wired' : 'stored-only'}</Chip> rendered next to every key with its consumer string as the tooltip), :576 (history rows suffixed ' · stored-only'), and :597 (an AI suggestion for an unwired key badged 'changes nothing').
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Either wire the nine `features.*` switches to the gates that already exist (the heartbeat and homeostasis both read comparable levers) or retire the dead keys from the stored config under §15.10, so the panel offers only controls that are controls.

### R4.8 · DELIVERED: the bespoke swarm cascade is genuinely user-reconfigurable end to end, with per-stage owned-model routing and an unhonoured-request report — **DELIVERED**

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** No tier — verified by execution, and the surface claims nothing it did not do. §6's "reconfigurable by users" clause is met for the cascade itself: I defined, ran, inspected and found a full define/edit/run/retire lifecycle with real persistence, real governance and real provenance, and the one disputable label (template vs. model planning) is badged on the surface rather than glossed.

A VSB-bound cascade prepends the living entity's current stage/mission/Chief via `_living_vsb_grounding` and reports `grounded_in_live_vsb`; it also refuses when the entity's Mode-3 review gates are closed. The page gives the whole lifecycle: stage add/remove/edit, its own context field, Save (POST) vs Edit (PUT), Run, and Retire behind a confirmation that names the side effect ("It is bound to <vsb>, so that entity's native_swarm pointer is cleared too"). The workflow tree is governed for real: `max_parallel` 4 was reduced to 3 by the homeostatic controller, and the tree reports its planner honestly — `planner: "deterministic_template"`, rendered as an amber "planned by template" badge whose tooltip says "the branch set adapts to words in the goal, which is not the same as reasoning about it".

- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Not refuted — I reproduced every mechanism it claims, so DELIVERED and tier 0 stand for the reconfiguration lifecycle this finding is about: define, edit, save, run, retire, with real persistence, a real hash-chained audit entry, live homeostatic registration, per-stage model routing and an unhonoured-request report. One sentence in its reasoning is wrong and I am recording it rather than letting it stand: 'the surface claims nothing it did not do' is false on this very route, because the trace and final it returns carry finding 0's false statement about the user's request (my run's final read 'Subject: Audit the design for risk ... Structured frame over the terms most frequent in the request ... - external dependency'). I keep tier 0 here rather than escalating this row to tier 1 because that defect is finding 0, on the same route, and tiering it twice would double-count one truth defect and inflate the region's tier-1 census — the correction belongs in finding 0's scope, which I widened there.

POST /api/v1/resources/swarm/run with three arbitrary role+instruction stages and my own context ran all three and returned run_id 'sr-d093603d', served_by_all ['native'], requests_not_honoured [], duration_ms 35, and audit {logged: true, ueg_hash: '746c9aab...f887'} — a full 128-hex SHA3-512 ledger entry reported as a fact. homeostasis came back live (max_parallel, posture 'protected', metabolic_load 0.44) with atp_measured false and its basis attached. The designer lifecycle is real in source: apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/developers/NativeAI.tsx:928-968 is the #cascade-designer card with stage role/instruction inputs, Add stage, Remove stage, its own context field, a Save disabled until a stage has both a role and an instruction, and an explicit 'Editing a saved cascade — Save updates it' mode with a way out; :1015 is the retire confirmation naming the side effect ('It is bound to <vsb_id>, so that entity's native_swarm pointer is cleared too'). ### R4.9 · DELIVERED: the native-AI fabric's measurement routes refuse to invent — nulls carry reasons, simulated terms are named, and unreachable thresholds are stated as unreachable — **DELIVERED**

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** No tier — this is the §15.6 invariant working, verified by executing each route and checking that no figure appears without a basis and no gate reports a pass it could not compute. The frontend badge helper has a genuine third state — absent provenance renders "provenance not recorded", and `served_by` placeholders like `real-engine`, `none` and `unavailable` each get their own honest label instead of an in-house-model badge.

I executed each route and checked the specific property this finding claims: no figure appears without a basis, and no gate reports a pass it could not compute. Several routes volunteer facts against their own interest (a threshold declared unreachable, a consensus recorded as three abstentions, a composite score published twice so the unmeasured terms cannot hide inside it), which is the §15.6 invariant working rather than a surface to be tiered. ## R5 — §1–§3, §3A, §9, §14, §15, §17.1 — the offerings, the avatar/UX, democratisation, the founding principles, the 4×6×4 grid

**Assessor's region summary:** R5 measured against Sections 1-3, 3A, 9, 14, 15, 17.1 and Appendix A.1/A.6, by executing ~35 routes against the live backend at 127.0.0.1:8031 and reading the components that render them.  WHAT IS GENUINELY DELIVERED. All six Domains offer real, reachable, in-house-AI-mediated tools: 26 tools across six routed hubs, each mounted from one registry (lib/toolRegistry.ts) that both front doors count from, so no advertised total is typed by hand. Every domain's catalogue route returns real reference data (5 madhabs, 10 research methodologies, 8 education frameworks, UK legal templates, care tools keyed to NHSI/BAPEN/NICE). The honesty machinery around these tools is the strongest thing in this region: one provenanceBadge helper that cannot let floor output wear the model badge and that names six non-model placeholder states individually; a tri-state QMS chip that renders "not assessable" rather than a green pass when the floor served; and withDisclosures(), which makes the floor note and the disclaimer travel into the copy, the save and all four download formats. Two deterministic engines do real work and state their own limits precisely: the NEWS2 scorer returns band:null, complete:false, a named missing list and "the total is a lower bound" when observations are absent, and the SM-2 memorisation engine's XAI route distinguishes the real arithmetic from its display-weight attribution. The Qur'an path is exemplary - Arabic retrieved from alquran.cloud with the Basmala separated and its basis stated, no scripture stored in any page, no fallback copy, and translation refusing with 503 by design. The avatar meets Section 9 in substance and discloses every modality it could not serve, with a different sentence for each reason. Accessibility and personalisation are partial and say so exactly: twelve languages offered, four actually translated, and the Settings copy states per-language coverage, the real string count, and that AI-generated content is still English. Realm is threaded into all ~22 domain-tool handlers and travels from the user's saved preference.  WHERE THE GAP IS. Two distinct classes. First, stale narrative literals on QEP surfaces that the rest of the page has outgrown: a roadmap card promising an emotion-adaptive interface, which A.9.4 ratifies as refused; a button offering an "Ethics Council" that exists in exactly one string in the repository; and click-toasts naming a "QEP Season 2" and a "Phase 4" that the delivery plan does not contain, while blaming the user's hardware for a feature with no code. Each sits beside prose that is scrupulously honest, which is what makes them easy to miss and worth fixing cheaply. Second, a structural asymmetry: the withhold-and-say-which discipline (floor_note + sections_withheld) is implemented in Religion only - 15 and 14 occurrences there, zero in all five other domain handlers - so Care, Law, Education, Science and Employment emit their promised professional headings filled with keyword bigrams and no word about why. Beyond that, the Products axis of the headline 4x6x4 grid has no code realisation at all (no PRODUCTS constant in either taxonomy module), so the product exposes a 4x6 grid; two working domain capabilities reach no UI; and the tool sets are not of comparable scale (7 vs 3).  I did NOT hit the ten-finding cap, so ten is close to the whole picture for this region at this resolution - but three qualifications. (1) I could not drive a browser, so every statement about what a user sees is reasoned from the component source, and I have named the file and line for each. (2) I executed one tool per domain plus the QEP and avatar surfaces; the remaining ~18 tools were read, not run, so the heading-with-no-floor-note finding is verified in four domains and inferred from zero grep hits in the fifth. (3) I treated A.9's six narrowings, A.10's exclusions and A.12's five pending rulings as ratified boundaries throughout and excluded them from the gap - the one place they appear is as the opposite problem, a surface advertising a refused capability.

### R5.0 · The Qur'an Education Platform's roadmap advertises an emotion-adaptive interface, which the vision ratifies as REFUSED — **PARTIAL** · tier 1

- **severity (assessor):** high
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The reader is shown a card titled "Adaptive UI" whose whole description is the sentence "Interface adjusts to age and emotion.", under a section heading that vouches for its own honesty ("Roadmap - modules not yet built (honestly labelled)"). That is an affirmative statement about what the platform intends to do, and it is untrue: A.9.4 records emotion inference as NOT ADOPTED, re-openable only by an Owner ruling with a consent model attached, for a population that includes minors. Not tier 2, because nothing here is a hidden shortfall - the card does not understate a capability, it announces one the constitution forbids. Not tier 3, because the "planned" badge discloses that the feature is absent, which is the opposite of the problem: it endorses it as coming. This is also the one item in the appendix where a surface promising the capability is worse than a surface lacking it.
- **claim:** A.9.4: "Emotion inference is not adopted ... QEP's adaptive UI adapts to profile and stated preference (the W428 explicit profile), never to inferred affect. Reopening this needs an Owner ruling with a consent model attached." A.6 feature 12's status names the W428 explicit profile as "the right substrate".
- **observed:** C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/QEPFlagshipFeatures.tsx:33 declares `{ id: 'adaptive_ui', name: 'Adaptive UI', ... desc: 'Interface adjusts to age and emotion.' }`. The grid is reached at /religion -> "QEP Flagship" tab -> the "Roadmap - modules not yet built (honestly labelled)" section (ReligionHub.tsx:115-128), and each card renders a 7px "planned" badge plus that description verbatim (lines 69-72). A repo-wide grep for "emotion" across apps/workstation-superapp/src returns this card and nothing else except AdaptiveUIProvider.tsx:13/48, where `emotionalAdjustment` is derived purely from the stored `ui.tone` preference, and six domain hubs that render it as "{tone} TONE (saved pref.)" with the tooltip "a saved preference - no request from this hub carries it yet". So the live mechanism is correctly preference-based; only the QEP roadmap card promises inference.
- **evidence:** QEPFlagshipFeatures.tsx:33 (the literal), ReligionHub.tsx:115-128 (reach), QEPFlagshipFeatures.tsx:69-72 (render), AdaptiveUIProvider.tsx:48 `emotionalAdjustment: ui.tone === 'neutral' ? 'Neutral' : 'Encouraging'` (the real, compliant mechanism), ScienceHub.tsx:31 (the honest "saved pref." badge).
- **disclosed to the user:** No - the opposite. The card is presented inside a section whose heading asserts the labels are honest, so a reader has no cue that this particular line describes something the platform has ruled out.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Reproduced in full, and the detail panel makes it worse than the assessor said. QEPFlagshipFeatures.tsx:33 carries the literal `desc: 'Interface adjusts to age and emotion.'`; it is reached at /religion -> QEP Flagship tab, rendered by ReligionHub.tsx:120-126 under the heading 'Roadmap - modules not yet built (honestly labelled)', with a 7px 'planned' badge (QEPFlagshipFeatures.tsx:69-72). I additionally read launchFeature (lines 46-51) and the expanded panel (lines 96-112): clicking the card opens 'Adaptive UI Module / Not yet built - nothing was run' over the sentence 'This module is described in the platform plan but has no backend yet' - so the surface does not merely describe emotion inference, it asserts it is IN THE PLAN. The vision's A.9.4 (line 1325) states the opposite in terms: 'Not adopted ... Reopening this needs an Owner ruling with a consent model attached', and line 1131 repeats 'add emotional state - not adopted'. I confirmed the live mechanism is compliant: AdaptiveUIProvider.tsx:48 derives emotionalAdjustment purely from the stored ui.tone preference, fontScale is a real rendered effect, and six hubs label it '{tone} TONE (saved pref.)' with the tooltip 'no request from this hub carries it yet' - which is why the verdict is PARTIAL (the A.9.4-compliant adaptive UI IS live) and not MISSING. Tier 1 and not tier 2: nothing here understates a capability, so there is no hidden shortfall - the card and its panel make an affirmative, untrue statement about what the platform intends, for a population that includes minors. Tier 1 and not tier 3: the 'planned' badge discloses only that the feature is absent, which endorses it as coming rather than correcting it; a disclosure that the thing is on its way is not a disclosure that it has been refused.
- **refuter's evidence:** apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/QEPFlagshipFeatures.tsx:33 (literal), :46-51 (launchFeature sets 'not yet built'), :69-72 (planned badge + desc render), :96-112 ('described in the platform plan'); apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/domains/ReligionHub.tsx:120-126 (reach); apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/AdaptiveUIProvider.tsx:44-58 (preference-only mechanism); docs/WORKSTATION_IDBO_WHOLE_VISION.md:1325 (A.9.4), :1131; repo grep for 'emotion' in apps/workstation-superapp/src returns only this card, the provider, and the six 'saved pref.' badges.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Change that one `desc` string to the A.9.4 posture, e.g. "Adapts to your stated profile and preferences (text size, guided mode, tone) - never to inferred emotion, which is not adopted." No other code changes.

### R5.1 · The Religion hub's primary ethics call-to-action invites the user to consult an "Ethics Council" that exists nowhere and opens the AI CEO hub instead — **STUB** · tier 1

- **severity (assessor):** high
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The reader is shown a full-width primary button reading "Consult Ethics Council", sitting directly beneath a paragraph that correctly says "A ruling belongs to a qualified scholar, not to a screen." The button asserts that an Ethics Council exists and is consultable. It does not: a repo-wide grep for "Ethics Council" / "ethics_council" / "EthicsCouncil" across apps/workstation-superapp/src and agentic_core returns exactly one hit - this button label - and pages/council/ is an empty directory. Clicking navigates to /ceo, the Living Organisation hub (AI CEO / Board / Swarm). Not tier 2, because there is no partial capability being under-described - the named body does not exist at all, so the untruth is the claim of its existence, not a hidden gap. Not tier 3, because nothing on the card, the button or the destination says there is no such council; and in the Religion domain this is precisely the A.9.6 shape (an AI channel offered where a verified-human body is named).
- **claim:** A.9.6: "'Ask a Scholar' requires actual scholars. It is a verified-human channel, not an AI persona. Until real scholars are verified, the surface must say so - as it already does." Section 9 (Owner, W86-W88/W127-W142): "only wired, functional surfaces are routed (aspirational pages archived, never shown)". Principle 10: "stubs and mocks never ship".
- **observed:** C:/Users/rehan/Workstation/apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/domains/ReligionHub.tsx:349: `<Button onClick={() => navigate('/ceo')} className="w-full bg-aura text-sovereign py-6 ...">Consult Ethics Council</Button>`. The same card's prose (lines 335-344) is exemplary - it explains the Section 11 screens are word lists that "FLAG terms for review", that "an unmatched list means nothing matched, never 'no concerns'", and that a ruling belongs to a scholar. The button then offers the ruling-giving body the prose has just disclaimed. The header of the same hub also carries "Ethical Guidance Channel" and a second button "Seek Guidance" -> /ceo (ReligionHub.tsx:38, 51). App.tsx:172 maps /ceo to LivingOrganisationHub; nothing there is an ethics council.
- **evidence:** ReligionHub.tsx:349 (the button), ReligionHub.tsx:335-344 (the contradicted prose), App.tsx:172 `<Route path="/ceo" element={<LivingOrganisationHub />} />`, empty directory apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/council/, zero grep hits for the name anywhere else in apps/ or agentic_core/.
- **disclosed to the user:** No. Neither the button, nor its card, nor the /ceo destination says that no ethics council exists or that the destination is the AI CEO.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Reproduced, and the named body does not exist anywhere. ReligionHub.tsx:349 renders a full-width primary button 'Consult Ethics Council' -> navigate('/ceo'); App.tsx:172 maps /ceo to LivingOrganisationHub, a 55-line file in which a grep for 'ethic' returns ZERO hits; apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/council/ is empty; a grep for 'Ethics Council|ethics_council|EthicsCouncil' across apps/ and agentic_core/ returns exactly one hit - this button's label. I also grepped docs/WORKSTATION_IDBO_WHOLE_VISION.md for 'Ethics Council|Ethics Board': zero - so the body is not even a vision aspiration the surface is anticipating, it is invented at the button. The same hub's header carries 'Ethical Guidance Channel' and a second button 'Seek Guidance' -> /ceo (lines 38, 51). STUB rather than MISSING: the defect is not an absent feature but a reached control that pretends a consultable body exists. Tier 1 and not tier 2: there is no partial capability being under-described - the untruth is the claim of existence itself, sitting immediately below the card's own correct sentence that 'A ruling belongs to a qualified scholar, not to a screen' (lines 338-344). Tier 1 and not tier 3: nothing on the button, the card or the /ceo destination says no such council exists, and this is precisely the A.9.6 shape (vision line 1334: 'Ask a Scholar requires actual scholars ... a verified-human channel, not an AI persona') realised as a button that lands on the AI CEO.
- **refuter's evidence:** apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/domains/ReligionHub.tsx:349 (button), :38/:51 ('Ethical Guidance Channel', 'Seek Guidance'), :338-344 (the contradicted prose); apps/workstation-superapp/src/App.tsx:172; wc -l LivingOrganisationHub.tsx = 55 with zero 'ethic' hits; empty apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/council/; docs/WORKSTATION_IDBO_WHOLE_VISION.md:1334 (A.9.6), zero hits for 'Ethics Council'.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Relabel the button for what it opens, e.g. "Open the Living Organisation (AI CEO)" - or, following Principle 10's first-class resolution, remove it and leave the card's honest paragraph, which already tells the user where a ruling comes from.

### R5.2 · The QEP flagship's two unbuilt tabs answer a click with a release name and a hardware requirement that nothing in the system backs — **STUB** · tier 1

- **severity (assessor):** medium
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** Three specific sentences are shown to a reader on click. "AR Mouth Model requires a WebXR-compatible device - coming in Phase 4" and "VR Mosque requires a WebXR headset - coming in Phase 4" attribute the unavailability to the reader's hardware - so a user who IS on a WebXR headset is told something plainly false about why nothing happened - and name a phase that does not contain this work (the plan's own line is "P4 is the Owner's hand", the Owner-switch phase; grep for webxr/AR-VR in docs/FABLE_DELIVERY_PROMPT.md returns zero). Not tier 2: the absence of the capability IS disclosed (PLANNED badges, 0 participants, and prose saying "no WebXR code exists yet"), so what remains is not a hidden shortfall but three positive claims that are untrue. Not tier 3 for the same reason - the untrue parts are asserted, not omitted.

The surrounding prose is honest - "Immersive Lab - planned (Phase 4)", "no WebXR code exists yet, so nothing here claims readiness", three cards badged PLANNED with "0 Participants" - so the toasts are the only untrue text on the two tabs.
- **evidence:** QEPReligionHub.tsx:234, :218, :219 (the three toast literals); QEPReligionHub.tsx:20-25 (the two tabs); docs/FABLE_DELIVERY_PROMPT.md:723 "P4 is the Owner's hand" and :815 "P4 0/6" - a case-insensitive grep of that file for webxr|AR/VR|immersive|arvr|Season 2 returns nothing.

All three toast literals reproduced at QEPReligionHub.tsx:220, :221, :234, on two of the flagship's five top-level tabs (lines 19-25: 'Competitions', 'AR/VR Lab'). A case-insensitive repo grep for 'Season 2' returns only this toast and an _archive/ copy - nothing in agentic_core, nothing in the delivery plan; a grep for 'webxr' returns this component, the excluded long-form docs/QURAN_EDUCATION_PLATFORM_VISION.md (whose technology and roadmap parts A.10 bars from being read as a vision claim), and two _archive/ files. docs/FABLE_DELIVERY_PROMPT.md:723 reads 'P4 is the Owner's hand' and contains no WebXR/AR-VR work, so 'coming in Phase 4' names a phase that does not hold it. Tier 1 and not tier 2: the ABSENCE is disclosed well - the card prose says 'no WebXR code exists yet, so nothing here claims readiness' (line 215) and the tournament cards badge PLANNED - so what is left is not a hidden shortfall but three positive claims that are untrue. The strongest leg is 'Global leaderboard launching with QEP Season 2': it is an invented release commitment with no counter-statement anywhere on the Competitions tab. The AR/VR pair adds a false CAUSE - a reader on a WebXR headset is told their device is the blocker. Tier 1 and not tier 3: the untrue parts are asserted on click, not omitted, and a tier-3 reading would require the surface to state them as unbacked, which nothing does.
- **refuter's evidence:** apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/domains/QEPReligionHub.tsx:234, :220, :221 (toasts), :19-25 (tabs), :205-222 (the honest ARVRLab prose), :226-236 (PLANNED cards); docs/FABLE_DELIVERY_PROMPT.md:723; repo-wide 'Season 2'/'webxr' greps (only this file, _archive/, and the A.10-excluded long-form QEP doc).
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Replace the three toast strings with what is true: "No tournament or leaderboard backend exists - this is a preview" and "No WebXR code exists yet; this is not blocked by your device." ### R5.3 · Five of the six domains emit their promised professional section headings on the floor with nothing in them and no floor note, while Religion withholds them and says which — **PARTIAL** · tier 3 *(assessed tier 2)*

- **severity (assessor):** high
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** A user who runs the Employment "Application Form & Supporting Statement" tool gets a response whose body contains the heading "## Criteria Coverage Check" followed by one line: "- medical director". The heading asserts that a criterion-by-criterion coverage check was performed against the person specification - the specification was in the request and is absent from the output entirely. Same shape in Care ("## Risk Management Plan", "## Crisis Plan"), Education ("## Assessment Points", "## Differentiation"), Law ("## Relevant Law", "## Conclusion") and Science ("## Native approach"). The platform already built the honest answer for this exact case - Religion's four tools return `floor_note` and `sections_withheld` and the hub prints both - and five domains do not. Not tier 1: the response does disclose the floor (the body opens "[Workstation native structured engine ...]", says it is "framing the request above rather than analysing it", and DomainTool paints an amber badge "structured floor - not model analysis" plus a "QMS -" chip whose tooltip says no instrument can judge the content), so nothing certifies the content as good. Not tier 3: a reader is never told that the named sections were not produced, which is the specific fact Religion's own tools state - the shortfall is real and the surface is silent about it.
- **claim:** Section 3A: the six domains carry tool sets "of comparable scope, scale and complexity", and "the application-form / supporting-statement tool ... never invents experience the candidate lacks". Principle 6: never fabricate; label simulation. Section 10/11 as realised in the Religion tools: withhold the section and say which.
- **observed:** Executed live against http://127.0.0.1:8031. `grep -c floor_note` over the six handlers: religion 15, science 0, education 0, law 0, employment 0, care 0; `sections_withheld`: religion 14, all others 0. POST /api/v1/employment/application {target_role:"Medical Director", person_specification:"Essential: 10 years NHS board-level clinical governance...", experience:"6 months as a supermarket shelf-stacker"} -> 200 with statement = "## Supporting Statement\n... - medical director\n\n## Criteria Coverage Check\n... - medical director" and no floor_note, no sections_withheld. POST /api/v1/care/care-plan and /api/v1/law/research returned the same shape. The one genuinely good news: the floor invents NOTHING - it did not award the shelf-stacker any clinical governance experience, so the Owner's never-invent directive holds.
- **evidence:** agentic_core/api/religion.py (floor_note x15, sections_withheld x14) vs agentic_core/api/{science,education,law,employment,care}.py (zero of each); live responses saved at C:/Users/rehan/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/r5/{app,care,law,edu,emp,sci}.json; ReligionHub.tsx:143-151 and :271-279 (the renderExtra that prints floor_note + "Withheld on the floor: ..."); DomainTool.tsx:314-321 (renderExtra is the only on-screen path for floor_note, so a domain that does not send one shows nothing).
- **disclosed to the user:** No for the specific sections. The floor itself IS disclosed (body banner, amber provenance badge, QMS tooltip, and the export header via provenanceLine), but no surface says that "Criteria Coverage Check" or "Risk Management Plan" was not produced.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). The ASYMMETRY is real and I reproduced it live, but the assessor's load-bearing claim ('the heading asserts a check was performed', disclosed=No) does not survive reading the actual body, so the tier drops from 2 to 3. Counts confirmed: floor_note/sections_withheld = 15/14 in agentic_core/api/religion.py and 0/0 in each of science, education, law, employment, care. POST /api/v1/employment/application with person_specification='Essential: 10 years NHS board-level clinical governance...' and experience='6 months as a supermarket shelf-stacker' -> 200, no floor_note, no sections_withheld; the floor invented nothing (the shelf-stacker was awarded no governance experience), so the Owner's never-invent directive holds. POST /api/v1/law/research (the assessor's payload 422s - the field is `question`, not `legal_question`) -> 200, same shape, no floor_note. But every printed section carries its own disclosure immediately under the heading: "Native structured frame for 'Criteria Coverage Check' over: Medical Director (domain: the stated domain). The list below is the request's most frequent terms, not findings about them." That sentence tells the reader, at the heading, that no coverage check was performed - which is the specific fact the assessor said was never stated. Tier 3 and not tier 2: by the stated rule, a shortfall the surface itself states is tier 3 however large, and this output surface states it three ways (the engine banner, the per-section 'not findings about them' line, and DomainTool's amber 'structured floor - not model analysis' badge with its 'QMS -' chip). Tier 3 and not tier 1: nothing certifies the content. What IS genuinely undisclosed, and would merit tier 2 if filed as its own finding, is the PRE-RUN card copy: EmploymentHub.tsx:111 promises 'a criterion-by-criterion supporting statement' with no floor qualifier, where Religion's registry entries were fixed under W498 to carry one ('on the floor the sources and the school position are withheld'). The register row should carry that leg, because it is the half the assessor mis-sited.
- **refuter's evidence:** grep -c floor_note: religion 15 / science 0 / education 0 / law 0 / employment 0 / care 0; sections_withheld: religion 14, others 0. Live: POST /api/v1/employment/application 200 (body: '## Criteria Coverage Check' + "the request's most frequent terms, not findings about them" + '- medical director'), POST /api/v1/law/research 200 (IRAC headings, same per-section sentence, no floor_note), POST /api/v1/religion/fatwa-research 200 (floor_note 'NO jurisprudential research happened ... withheld rather than shown as a frame', sections_withheld = 7 named sections). apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/domains/EmploymentHub.tsx:111 (the unqualified 'criterion-by-criterion' promise); apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/toolRegistry.ts:25,28 (Religion's qualified copy); DomainTool.tsx:280-321 (badge, QMS chip, disclaimer render).
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Apply the Religion pattern to the other five handlers: when provenance.served_by is native/template, drop the sections the floor cannot produce and return `sections_withheld` plus a `floor_note`. The frontend needs no change for the note (DomainTool already exports it) beyond adding the same two-line renderExtra the Religion tabs use.

### R5.4 · Every floor-served domain output opens by asserting a professional persona - "Acting as: Islamic scholar and Quranic exegete", "registered nurse", "senior solicitor" - often truncated mid-word, and that line travels into every export — **PARTIAL** · tier 2

- **severity (assessor):** medium
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The second line of the Qur'anic Tafsir result a user reads is "_Acting as: Islamic scholar and Quranic exegete._", immediately above study notes on Surah al-Fatihah. The same payload's disclaimer says "NOT a scholarly tafsir". One of those two statements is false, and the false one comes first and is unqualified. The same leak puts "Acting as: senior care coordinator and registered nurse" above a care plan and "Acting as: clinical risk assessment specialist supporting a qualified cl." (truncated at the engine's 140-char field cap) above a NEWS2 interpretation. Not tier 1: the adjacent floor banner and the disclaimer in the same response do tell the reader this is a deterministic engine and not scholarship, so a reader who reads the whole payload is not left believing a scholar wrote it, and no figure is fabricated. Not tier 3: nothing anywhere says the "Acting as" line is a prompt artefact rather than a statement about the author, so the specific false claim is undisclosed - and it is copied into every export, because DomainTool's withDisclosures() composes the body verbatim.
- **claim:** Section 11 / A.9 as the appendix frames it - AI content is "labelled AI-assisted with serving provenance - not authoritative"; A.9.1's precedent that a fabricated claim of religious authority "would be a false witness". Principle 6: honesty over polish. taxonomy.py's own realm-directive comment records this engine hazard: engine.py:90 `_role` takes the FIRST persona match and engine.py:81 `_field` caps a value at 140 chars.
- **observed:** Executed live. POST /api/v1/religion/quran-tafsir {surah:1, ayah_start:1, ayah_end:3} -> `tafsir` begins "_[Workstation native structured engine - owned, no external dependency]_\n\n_Acting as: Islamic scholar and Quranic exegete._" while the same response's `disclaimer` reads "AI-assisted study aid - NOT a scholarly tafsir and NOT a ruling" and its `floor_note` reads "the study notes below are a structured frame, not scholarship". POST /api/v1/law/research -> "_Acting as: senior England and Wales solicitor specialising in housing la._" (truncated). POST /api/v1/care/risk-assess -> "_Acting as: clinical risk assessment specialist supporting a qualified cl._". The same outputs also leak raw prompt text under the headings, e.g. the care plan's "## My Goals" section contains "Produce a care plan with these sections:\n## My Goals (in the person's own voice - what they w" and the risk assessment contains "COMPUTED SCORE (authoritative - computed in-house from the published table; do NOT " cut mid-sentence.
- **evidence:** Live responses at C:/Users/rehan/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/r5/{rel,law,careblank,riskblank}.json; agentic_core/taxonomy.py:28-36 (the comment documenting engine.py:90 `_role` and engine.py:81 `_field`'s 140-char cap, which is why the personas truncate); apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/DomainTool.tsx:78-82 `withDisclosures` (the body is composed verbatim into the save, the copy and all four download formats).
- **disclosed to the user:** No for the persona line itself. The floor is disclosed (banner, badge, export provenance header), and the Religion case even carries an explicit "not scholarship" disclaimer - but nothing marks the "Acting as" sentence as a prompt echo rather than an authorship claim.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Reproduced verbatim, including the truncations and a larger prompt leak than the assessor reported. POST /api/v1/religion/quran-tafsir {surah 1, ayah 1-3} -> the tafsir's second line is '_Acting as: Islamic scholar and Quranic exegete._' while the same payload's disclaimer reads 'AI-assisted study aid - NOT a scholarly tafsir and NOT a ruling'. POST /api/v1/law/research -> '_Acting as: senior England and Wales solicitor specialising in housing la._' (cut mid-word at the engine's 140-char field cap). POST /api/v1/care/care-plan -> '_Acting as: senior care coordinator and registered nurse._', and each of its sections embeds raw prompt text as content: "Produce a care plan with these sections:\n## My Goals (in the person's own voice - what they want to achieve)\n## My Strengths and Support Network" repeated under every heading. POST /api/v1/religion/fatwa-research -> 'Acting as: Islamic studies scholar with expertise in classical jurisprud.'. I verified the travel path: DomainTool.tsx:79-83 withDisclosures composes body + floor_note + disclaimer into the save, the copy and every download, so the persona line AND the leaked prompt text are exported together with the disclaimers. Tier 2 and not tier 1: the line immediately ABOVE the persona is '_[Workstation native structured engine - owned, no external dependency]_', the rendered disclaimer (DomainTool.tsx:321) says 'NOT a scholarly tafsir', and the amber provenance badge says 'structured floor - not model analysis' - so a reader of the payload or the screen is not left believing a scholar authored it, and no figure is fabricated. Tier 2 and not tier 3: nothing anywhere identifies the 'Acting as' sentence, or the embedded prompt instructions, as prompt artefacts rather than output, so this specific false authorship claim is itself undisclosed - and in the sacred-text case it is the A.9.1 shape the vision warns about.
- **refuter's evidence:** Live bodies: /api/v1/religion/quran-tafsir ('Acting as: Islamic scholar and Quranic exegete.'), /api/v1/law/research ('...housing la.'), /api/v1/care/care-plan (persona + embedded 'Produce a care plan with these sections:' prompt text), /api/v1/religion/fatwa-research ('...classical jurisprud.'); agentic_core/taxonomy.py:28-36 (comment recording engine.py:90 _role first-match and engine.py:81's 140-char _field cap); apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/DomainTool.tsx:79-83 (withDisclosures), :321 (disclaimer render).
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Stop emitting the role line in floor-served output (it is an instruction to a model, not content). If it is kept for traceability, move it out of the body into the provenance block so it cannot be read as authorship, and so it stops being exported as part of the text.

### R5.5 · The Religion domain's offering-1 depth is four honest tools plus a real memorisation studio - roughly a quarter of Appendix A.6's register, with the rest disclosed as a roadmap — **PARTIAL** · tier 3

- **severity (assessor):** medium
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** This is the explicit A.1/A.6 depth question, and the shortfall is stated on the surface, so it is tier 3 however large it is. A user who opens /qep or /religion -> QEP Flagship sees the thirteen-card grid under the heading "Roadmap - modules not yet built (honestly labelled)", every card badged "planned" except Memorization which is badged "live", and a Competitions tab whose cards read PLANNED with 0 participants. The two capabilities that DO work say what they are and what they are not. Not tier 2: the gap is named at the surface, not hidden. Not tier 1: no card claims an outcome the platform did not produce - with the single exception already filed separately (the emotion card). I am reporting it as the measured depth, not as neglect: A.9's six narrowings and A.12's five pending rulings are ratified boundaries and I have excluded them from this count.
- **claim:** A.1: QEP is "the Religion domain's flagship instance of Section 3A offering 1 - domain-specific, in-house-AI-mediated tools and resources a user works with immediately", spanning all four Realms (a Learning user memorises, an Enterprise user runs a madrasah, a Developing user builds curricula, a Scholarship user researches tafsir). A.6 registers fifteen core features.
- **observed:** Measured against A.6, excluding the A.9 narrowings and A.12-blocked items. REAL and verified by execution: feature 3 (POST /api/v1/qep/hifz/schedule+review, GET /progress - real SM-2; GET /api/v1/qep/xai/explanations returns `interval_basis: "computed by the REAL MemorizationEngine (SM-2), not an approximation"` and names its display-weight attribution as an aid, not the engine's arithmetic); feature 4's XP half (GET /api/v1/qep/gamification/u1 returns `level_basis: "1 + xp // 100"`, `streak_basis`, `scope: "recorded awards only - nothing here is estimated"`); feature 2's written-recall narrowing; the sourced-Arabic path (GET /api/v1/qep/ayah/{s}/{a}, consumed by the Tajwid panel so no Arabic is stored in the page). ABSENT, and said to be: features 6 (Educator Platform), 7 (Community), 8 (Video Conferencing), 4's competitions/leaderboards/badges, 5's curricula, 3's flashcards/heatmap/audio, and feature 1's parent and teacher roles. Honest empties confirmed live: GET /api/qep/analytics/overview -> `{"measured": false, "metrics": {}}` with the reason; GET /api/v1/qep/compliance/audit -> `compliant: null` with three controls `not_checked` and the note "It is never asserted true on the strength of controls that do not run"; POST /api/v1/qep/translation/translate -> 503 by design (Section 11 rule 4). The four Realms are not differentiated in QEP: no madrasah/institution surface, no curriculum-builder surface, no tafsir-research surface - the realm travels with a request but changes nothing the floor can act on.
- **evidence:** Live: /api/v1/qep/status, /hifz/*, /gamification/u1, /xai/explanations, /compliance/audit, /api/qep/analytics/overview; apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/toolRegistry.ts:22-28 (Religion = 4 form tools + 1 flagship); QEPFlagshipFeatures.tsx:27-41 (the 13-card roadmap, 1 live / 12 planned); QEPReligionHub.tsx:226-236 (the PLANNED tournament cards).
- **disclosed to the user:** Yes - the roadmap grid, the PLANNED badges, the "Recitation assessment unavailable" panel, the LearnTeach surface's "no scholars are verified" posture, and the `measured: false` analytics all say so at the surface.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Re-measured by execution; every DELIVERED leg and every honest-empty the assessor cited is real, and the tier is right. GET /api/v1/qep/gamification/u1 -> {xp 0, level 1, level_basis '1 + xp // 100', streak_basis 'consecutive UTC days with >= 1 recorded award...', awards_recorded 0, scope 'recorded awards only - nothing here is estimated'}. GET /api/v1/qep/xai/explanations -> interval_basis 'computed by the REAL MemorizationEngine (SM-2), not an approximation' with per-feature rationales. GET /api/qep/analytics/overview -> {measured: false, metrics: {}} plus the reason that the UEG manager exposes only writes. GET /api/v1/qep/compliance/audit -> compliant: null with three controls not_checked, each naming why. POST /api/v1/qep/translation/translate -> 503 'the deterministic native floor cannot translate and its output will not be presented as a translation' - which the vision itself (line 1234) records as 'section 11 rule 4 working as designed', so it is a ratified boundary, not a shortfall. I also tested the four-Realms claim directly, which the assessor asserted but did not execute: POST quran-tafsir with realm='learning' and realm='scholarship' returned BYTE-IDENTICAL bodies (2042 chars each), so the realm genuinely changes nothing the floor acts on. Tier 3 and not tier 2: the gap is named at the surface - the thirteen-card grid sits under 'Roadmap - modules not yet built (honestly labelled)', twelve cards badged 'planned', the tournament cards PLANNED, and the analytics endpoint says measured: false. Tier 3 and not tier 1: no card claims an outcome the platform did not produce, with the single exception already filed as index 0. Correctly excludes the A.9 narrowings and A.12 rulings from the count.
- **refuter's evidence:** Live: /api/v1/qep/gamification/u1, /api/v1/qep/xai/explanations, /api/qep/analytics/overview, /api/v1/qep/compliance/audit, POST /api/v1/qep/translation/translate (503), POST /api/v1/religion/quran-tafsir x2 realms (identical output); apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/toolRegistry.ts:21-29 (Religion = 4 forms + 1 flagship); QEPFlagshipFeatures.tsx:27-41 (13 cards, 1 live); docs/WORKSTATION_IDBO_WHOLE_VISION.md:1234 (the 503 is rule 4 as designed).
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Nothing to fix for honesty. For depth, the Realm differentiation A.1 describes is the cheapest real gain: the four realm framings already exist in taxonomy.REALM_REGISTER, so a realm-aware QEP entry (madrasah / curriculum / research) would use machinery that is already built.

### R5.6 · The Products axis of the headline 4x6x4 grid has no code realisation, so the product exposes a 4x6 grid and not a 4x6x4 one — **MISSING** · tier 3

- **severity (assessor):** medium
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** Nobody is misled, because no user-facing surface claims the third axis: I grepped apps/workstation-superapp/src for "4 Realms", "4x6", "96", and a Reactor/Incubator/Factory/Laboratory picker and found only a comment in taxonomy.ts. The vision itself states the gap in the same paragraph that makes the claim, and names the Owner ruling that governs it. So this is a capability gap that is disclosed, not an invisible shortfall (tier 2) and not something told untruly to a user (tier 1).
- **claim:** Section 17.1: "The product grid - 4 Realms x 6 Domains x 4 Products (96 combinations), one lifecycle pattern" with "Products (how work runs): Reactor (rapid AI generation) - Incubator (iterative development) - Factory (production-grade delivery) - Laboratory (experimental/research)", and "All 96 follow the same Concept -> Design -> Build -> Launch -> Commercialise stage-gated lifecycle."
- **observed:** agentic_core/taxonomy.py declares REALMS (4) and DOMAINS (6) and nothing else - `grep PRODUCTS` returns zero hits in both taxonomy.py and apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/taxonomy.ts. The two realised axes are genuinely wired: realm is threaded as `realm=req.realm` into every one of the ~22 domain-tool handlers (religion, science, education, law, employment, care), DomainTool.tsx:124-128 sends the user's `defaultRealm` with every Offering-1 request, and taxonomy.REALM_REGISTER holds a real depth/register directive per realm. The four Products exist only as four separate developer pages (pages/developers/{DigitalReactor,Incubator,Factory,ForgePipeline}.tsx) that are not an axis over the Domains and feed no grid picker. GET /api/v1/catalog/products is a catalogue of platform SDKs and services, not this axis. I did not look for configs/realms.yaml.
- **evidence:** agentic_core/taxonomy.py (REALMS, DOMAINS, REALM_REGISTER, no PRODUCTS); apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/taxonomy.ts (REALMS, DOMAINS, WORKSPACE_DOMAINS, no PRODUCTS); agentic_core/api/{religion,science,education,law,employment,care}.py (realm threaded into every ai_text call); apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/DomainTool.tsx:124-128; zero frontend hits for the grid vocabulary.
- **disclosed to the user:** Not applicable to a user - no surface offers or mentions a Products axis. The vision discloses it in Section 17.1's own status note.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Confirmed, with one sub-claim of the finding corrected as too generous. agentic_core/taxonomy.py declares only REALMS (4 names, line 10) and DOMAINS (6, line 11); grep PRODUCTS returns zero in both taxonomy.py and apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/taxonomy.ts (which declares REALMS, DOMAINS, WORKSPACE_DOMAINS and nothing else). The vision's section 17.1 states the gap in its own status note - 'the Products AXIS has no code realisation - no PRODUCTS constant in the taxonomy, no grid picker feeding Genesis' - and names the Owner ruling (P3.0) that governs it, so this is disclosed, not an overclaim. The correction: the finding says 'the two realised axes are genuinely wired'. Realm is threaded into the handlers (religion 5, science 4, education 4, law 3, employment 6, care 4 occurrences of realm=req.realm), but I executed quran-tafsir with realm 'learning' and 'scholarship' and got byte-identical output, so on the floor the realm axis has NO observable effect - 'wired' overstates what a user gets. Tier 3 and not tier 2: no user-facing surface offers or names a Products axis (zero frontend hits for a Reactor/Incubator/Factory/Laboratory picker or the 4x6x4 grid vocabulary), so there is no partial claim left hidden. Tier 3 and not tier 1: nobody is told anything untrue, because nothing on a surface asserts the third axis. Worth recording for the register: the finding covers only HALF of section 17.1 - the same paragraph's second claim, 'All 96 follow the same Concept -> Design -> Build -> Launch -> Commercialise stage-gated lifecycle', carries its own status note that no single gated lifecycle exists and 219 of 219 VSBs hold stage 'commercialise' with no code path advancing it. That is the end-to-end lifecycle half of R5 and it is unexamined by this finding set.
- **refuter's evidence:** agentic_core/taxonomy.py:10-11 (REALMS, DOMAINS; no PRODUCTS); apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/taxonomy.ts:5-18; docs/WORKSTATION_IDBO_WHOLE_VISION.md:755-790 (section 17.1 and both status notes); realm=req.realm counts per handler; live identical-output test across two realms; pages/developers/{DigitalReactor,Incubator,Factory,ForgePipeline}.tsx exist as pages, not as an axis.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** This is the Owner's P3.0 ruling, not an engineering choice: either add PRODUCTS to agentic_core/taxonomy.py and taxonomy.ts and give one surface a product picker, or amend the Section 17.1 line to "design intent" so the canon stops describing an axis the product does not have.

POST /api/v1/science/hypothesis {research_question, domain agronomy, num_hypotheses 2, realm scholarship} -> 200, 1783 bytes. grep over apps/workstation-superapp/src: 'science/hypothesis' 0 hits, 'religion/interfaith' 0 hits, 'realms/v2' 0 hits; by contrast the three mounted science endpoints (synthesise, experiment-design, literature) have 4 frontend callers between them. Both handlers exist and are mounted: agentic_core/api/science.py:105 @router.post('/hypothesis'), agentic_core/api/religion.py:569 @router.post('/interfaith'). Tier 3 and not tier 1: the reached form is the response body, and the one I executed carries the same provenance block every mounted domain tool carries, including the qms basis stating that no instrument here can judge the content.
- **refuter's evidence:** Live: POST /api/v1/science/hypothesis 200/1783B; GET /api/realms/v2/discover 200 '[]'; zero-hit greps for 'science/hypothesis', 'religion/interfaith', 'realms/v2' over apps/workstation-superapp/src; agentic_core/api/science.py:105, agentic_core/api/religion.py:569; apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/toolRegistry.ts:31-35.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Add a `hypothesis` tab to ScienceHub and an `interfaith` tab to ReligionHub as DomainTool forms, and add both to toolRegistry.ts (the suite already fails when a hub and the registry disagree). That also narrows the parity spread in the next finding by two.

### R5.8 · The six domains' tool sets are not of comparable scale - Employment carries seven, Science and Law three — **PARTIAL** · tier 3

- **severity (assessor):** low
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The counts are on the surface: each card on /domains renders "{n} tools" straight from the one registry, so a user sees 3 beside Science and 7 beside Employment without being told anything false. That makes it a disclosed gap against a named Owner directive, not an invisible one. Not tier 2 because the disparity is literally displayed; not tier 1 because the front door's totals are computed from the registry rather than typed (the W470 fix), so no count is fabricated - I verified TOOL_TOTAL is a reduce over the registry and that AIToolsCatalogue derives its form/surface split the same way.
- **claim:** Section 3A, Owner directives recorded W446: "the six domains carry tool sets of comparable scope, scale and complexity (the Employment parity directive, incl. the application-form / supporting-statement tool that never invents experience the candidate lacks)".
- **observed:** From apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/toolRegistry.ts, the single source both front doors count from: Employment 7 (Application Studio + 6 forms), Religion 5 (4 forms + the QEP flagship), Education 4, Care 4, Science 3, Law 3 - 26 in total, a 2.3x spread between the largest and smallest. The parity directive was written to raise Employment, and Employment is now the outlier in the other direction. The backend bears this out: six distinct /api/v1/employment/* generators plus four /api/v1/career/* routes, against three each for science and law. Every domain's catalogue route executes and returns real reference data (religion/schools 5 madhabs, science/methodologies 10, education/frameworks 8, law/templates, care/tools keyed to NHSI/BAPEN/NICE standards), so no domain is a shell.
- **evidence:** apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/toolRegistry.ts:21-64 (the registry) and :66 `TOOL_TOTAL = TOOL_REGISTRY.reduce(...)`; DomainsHub.tsx:80 renders `{toolsOf(d.route).length} tools` per card; AIToolsCatalogue.tsx:19-20 derives FORMS/SURFACES from the registry; live GETs of /api/v1/{religion/schools, science/methodologies, education/frameworks, law/templates, care/tools} all 200 with real content.
- **disclosed to the user:** Yes - the per-domain counts are displayed on the Domains front door and in the AI Tools catalogue, both computed from the registry.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Counted from the registry myself: Employment 7 (Application Studio + 6 forms), Religion 5 (4 forms + the QEP flagship), Education 4, Care 4, Science 3, Law 3 = 26, a 2.3x spread, and the parity directive is a real vision claim (docs/WORKSTATION_IDBO_WHOLE_VISION.md:129 - 'sets of comparable scope, scale and complexity (the Employment parity directive, incl. the application-form / supporting-statement tool...)'). I verified the counts are COMPUTED, not typed: toolRegistry.ts:66 TOOL_TOTAL is a reduce over the registry, DomainsHub.tsx:14 toolsOf(route) is a lookup in that same registry and line 74 renders '{toolsOf(d.route).length} tools' per card, line 49 renders '{TOTAL} tools across {DOMAINS.length} domains - counted from the one tool registry every hub mounts from', and AIToolsCatalogue.tsx:19-20 derives FORMS/SURFACES by filtering the same array. DomainsHub.tsx:17 even builds each card's blurb from the tool titles, so a card cannot name a tool the hub does not mount. Tier 3 and not tier 2: the disparity is literally displayed - a user sees '3 tools' beside Science and '7 tools' beside Employment on /domains. Tier 3 and not tier 1: no count is fabricated; every figure on both front doors reduces over the single registry. Note the direction of the breach - the directive was written to RAISE Employment, and Employment is now the outlier the other way.
- **refuter's evidence:** apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/toolRegistry.ts:21-64 (the six tool lists), :66 (TOOL_TOTAL reduce); apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/domains/DomainsHub.tsx:14, :17, :49, :74; apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/AIToolsCatalogue.tsx:16-20; docs/WORKSTATION_IDBO_WHOLE_VISION.md:129.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** Wire the two API_ONLY tools above (Science +1, Religion +1) and add the obvious missing Law and Science forms rather than adding more Employment surface - or, if the spread is accepted, the directive line in Section 3A should say that parity means every domain carries a usable set, not an equal one.

### R5.9 · DELIVERED: the avatar is genuinely multimodal, enterprise-aware and voice-capable, and it says in plain words each thing it could not do — **PARTIAL** *(assessed DELIVERED)* · tier 3 *(assessed tier 0)*

- **severity (assessor):** none
- **why it is this tier (assessor):** No tier. Verified by execution and by reading the component that renders each field. The floor-served environment is disclosed rather than papered over, which is exactly the assessable property: the surface says the floor served it.
- **claim:** Section 9: "Enterprise-aware avatar integrated across the platform: multimodal communication / interaction (text - voice - image) with the AI CEO and the Chief, and guidance/navigation through any platform area" and the W53/E6-E7 ruling that "multimodality is in-house-first and honesty-bound ... otherwise an honest 'image received but not analysed' - never a fabricated description; voice runs in the browser (Web Speech, feature-detected - no fake microphone) ... when the serving resource cannot honour the language, the answer says so."
- **observed:** GET /api/v1/avatar/status -> `effective_serving_mode: "deterministic floor"` with a basis sentence naming AI_ALLOW_EXTERNAL as off and stating "the floor always answers, and it cannot translate or reason like a model"; it also says of the detected key "presence of the env var only - validity is only known at call time". POST /api/v1/avatar/chat with a 1x1 PNG -> `image_understood: false, image_status: "blocked_by_policy"`, and ConversationPanel.tsx renders that exact state as "The image was received and NOT sent anywhere: an external vision provider is configured but external use is switched off, so it was not read" - a different sentence from the failed_external and no-model cases. With `language:"ar"` -> `language_note: "Answered in English - your language needs the owned model. You asked for ar, and the deterministic floor served this answer; it cannot translate."` With `vsb_id` -> `grounded_in: "vsb-9313233561"` and the reply's term list visibly drew on that VSB's own concept text ("affordable solar-powered", "shared cold-store"), so the enterprise context really reaches the prompt; the backend's grounding block includes the Chief's title. Guided navigation is real and whitelisted: "What does the Genesis journey do?" returned `suggested_areas: [{route:"/genesis", because:"matched: genesis, journey"}]` and "my enterprise" returned the VSB Cockpit. POST /api/v1/avatar/speak refuses with "External voice output is unavailable ... The browser's speechSynthesis is the in-house path - nothing was sent externally", and useAvatarSession.ts uses window.speechSynthesis and window.SpeechRecognition directly, feature-detected. profile_applied is surfaced as "profile: not usable by the floor" rather than silently dropped.
- **evidence:** Live: /api/v1/avatar/{status,chat,speak}; apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/avatar/ConversationPanel.tsx:106-137 (image/profile/language disclosure blocks, each with its own data-testid) and :183-199 (image attach + spoken-replies controls); apps/workstation-superapp/src/hooks/useAvatarSession.ts:178-187, :278-294 (browser-native TTS/STT), :41-44 (resolveGroundingVsb), :256-260; agentic_core/avatars/api.py:50 (the ceo context prompt), :192-247 (the VSB grounding block incl. the Chief); Shell.tsx:11-13, 190, 283-291 (the avatar is app-wide).
- **disclosed to the user:** Yes, specifically and per-case: three distinct image-failure sentences, a language note naming the language asked for, a profile note naming the floor as the reason, and a provenance badge that routes through one helper so floor output can never wear the model badge.
- **refutation: REFUTED — corrected to PARTIAL.** The substance of the avatar claim verified, but the ONE sentence the finding held up as proof of honesty is inaccurate in this very environment, so DELIVERED/tier 0 is the wrong verdict and I correct it to PARTIAL. GET /api/v1/avatar/status returns effective_serving_mode_basis = 'no owned local model is reachable and, although an external key is present, external use is NOT permitted (AI_ALLOW_EXTERNAL is off)...' - while the SAME response says ollama_online: true and local_serving_disabled: true. Ollama answered its tags endpoint; it is disabled by flag, not unreachable. I read the computation (agentic_core/avatars/api.py:498-514): the else-branch's _why string chooses only on _key_present and never mentions _local_off, so the stated CAUSE is wrong whenever AI_DISABLE_LOCAL is set with Ollama up - a basis string asserting rather than computing from the state it already holds. Everything else I re-executed and it holds: POST /api/v1/avatar/chat with image_base64 -> image_understood false, image_status 'blocked_by_policy' (a distinct state from failed_external / no_vision_model), rendered by ConversationPanel.tsx:106-137 as its own sentence; language 'ar' -> language_note 'Answered in English - your language needs the owned model. You asked for ar, and the deterministic floor served this answer; it cannot translate.'; served_by 'native', is_external false, and the reply opens with the engine banner and labels its term list 'Extracted by counting words and adjacent pairs - not an analysis of the subject'; guided navigation real and whitelisted ('What does the Genesis journey do?' -> suggested_areas [{route /genesis, because 'matched: genesis, journey'}]); POST /api/v1/avatar/speak -> 503 'External voice output is unavailable ... The browser's speechSynthesis is the in-house path - nothing was sent externally'. I did NOT re-test the vsb_id grounding leg, so I neither confirm nor dispute it. Tier 3 and not tier 2: the corrective facts sit in the same response object (local_serving_disabled: true, ollama_online: true), and no frontend file references effective_serving_mode or its basis at all (zero grep hits across apps/workstation-superapp/src), so this is a field nobody reaches. Tier 3 and not tier 1: a reader of the only reached form - the JSON body - is handed the true state beside the wrong sentence, and the floor-served posture itself is disclosed correctly, which is the assessable property in this environment.
- **refuter's evidence:** Live GET /api/v1/avatar/status (basis sentence vs ollama_online true / local_serving_disabled true); agentic_core/avatars/api.py:498-514 (the _why branch ignores _local_off); live POST /api/v1/avatar/chat (image_status 'blocked_by_policy', language_note, served_by 'native', suggested_areas with 'because'), POST /api/v1/avatar/speak 503; apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/avatar/ConversationPanel.tsx:106-137; zero frontend hits for 'effective_serving_mode'.
- **smallest honest fix (assessor's proposal — a lead, not a decision):** None required. One small addition would help: `grounded_in` is returned and never rendered, so a user is not told their answer was grounded in their enterprise - an omission, not a defect, but a one-line win.
